Chapter 1: Introduction
Welcome to Crucible
Crucible is a Live Action Role Playing game where
participants play characters in a high fantasy world of
humans, elves, dwarves and far more peoples even
more fantastical as they battle magical creatures, the
undead, and the forces of the elements themselves.
The settings is a continuous story where Player
Characters gain strength, wealth and knowledge over
time against ever changing threats and challenges.
Most Important Rules
The following rules are the most important part of
this game. No Player, member of Staff or any other
participant in the game may violate, change, or limit
these rules, even by exception.
Personal Contact
Due to the nature of the game, all participants
consent to limited forms of physical contact such as
being struck by soft foam representations of
"Weapons" as well as by "Packets", which are small
cloth pouches filled with bird seed or similar filler.
Additionally, when skills require one player to touch
another, they must do so in the least intrusive way
possible, such as on the shoulder or arm. All such
contact should be made with a packet whenever
possible.
Contact is considered inappropriate if at least one
of the involved parties is uncomfortable. The reverse
is also true, where parties are free to engage in
consensual contact so long as neither party puts the
other at risk of injury and local laws are respected.
Hold
This is a special call that any person can make
when there is any perceived risk of injury or there are
other concerns which necessitate the game to
temporarily stop. Whenever a hold is called all
participants must stop where they are, what they're
doing and should not speak until the situation is
resolved. It is not acceptable to call a hold to check a
rule or ensure a player was affected by an ability
unless doing so is vital to all those involved in the
encounter.
When the reason for the hold has been resolved, a
marshal will call for the game to resume, usually with
the statement; "3, 2, 1 Game on!".
Fairness of Play
This game requires all Players to be honest in play
and treat all other Players with fairness. This means
properly counting your hits, not overcasting spells,
not using abilities your Character doesn't have, and
general honesty in playing the game.
Additionally, everyone is expected to not
deliberately play in a way that prevents another from
enjoying the game. Any player who deliberately
cheats, or who's presence is a consistent detriment to
others' enjoyment of the game will be banned from
further attendance.
In Character, you can lie and cheat all you want,
but when a Marshal asks you out of game if you stole
an item you must answer honestly.
Theft
Due to various restrictions in place by real world
governments and the owners of various game sites
there are a number of limitations on how a player can
be robbed. Generally speaking it is only possible to
steal from a designated in-game area and with the
victim's player's knowledge. Special "Thievery
Marshals" exist for when a Character wants to rob a
cabin without its occupants. It is not permitted to
"pickpocket" a player, because there is no way to
verify that the item being stolen is in game, but it is
acceptable to incapacitate and steal from the
character while the victim's player is able to confirm
what items are available to be stolen.
Metagaming
Almost every Player knows things their Character
does not. Just because a Player knows exactly how
much health a greater fire elemental has does not
mean that their potato farmer Character has this
information. In general, information which is not
included in the Setting Manual, or learned in game, is
not known by any Character. Information that a
Player learns Out of Game is not known by, and
cannot be acted upon by, their Character.
Game Marshals
There are a variety of Marshals who run different
parts of the game. From Weapon Marshals who
inspect all weapons to ensure their safety to Ritual
Marshals who approve, inspect and issue tags for
Ritual Items. Marshals do their best to ensure all
rules are followed but may make a mistake in the
moment. In any case it is never acceptable to argue
with a Marshal even if you are sure you are right. If
there is a question of a rule, it can be submitted to the
local chapter staff who will check with the Crucible
Rules Team for the final say.
This Rulebook
This is the Extended version of the Crucible
Rulebook. Its length is the result of many rules being
found in multiple places as well as including a
complete list of all standard rules and effects. Any
changes, clarifications, errata, or additions to the
Crucible rule set will be amended to this Rulebook on
a quarterly basis.
Understanding the Rulebook
When reading this rulebook any text which is
Italicized refers to a specific in game mechanic or
Skill. Words which are always capitalized refer to
general game effects. See below for an example.
Cure Wounds
School: Restoration
Level: 2
Range: Packet
Duration: Instant
Effect: This Spell causes ten(10) points of Magic
Healing to the target. Any healing above the
Character's maximum Hit Point total is lost.
Incantation: "I call upon the Dawn to Cure Wounds."
Delivery Verbal: "10 Magic Healing"
The highlighted area should be read as follows;
● This effect is a Spell.
● This effect causes ten(10) points of Healing.
● The Healing from this effect is delivered as
Magic. Any Defensive Abilities which are
applicable to effects delivered as Magic can
be used against this effect.
● Finally, the effect reminds the reader that any
healing above the Character's maximum Hit
Point total is lost.
Example 2
An additional example would be "magic", "Magic" and
"Magic".
• "magic" is simply a common term with no
special meaning beyond its normal usage.
• "Magic" refers to Spells, the rules around
casting Spells and Magic Skills.
• "Magic" refers to the Delivery Type used by
various Skills and abilities and included as
part of their Delivery Verbal.
You the Player
Each player is expected to be honest, follow all
rules to the best of their ability and play in a way that
is fun for other people. You may enjoy being a thief
but having their stuff stolen can be very discouraging
for other players and having it happen repeatedly
can, and has, made people feel the game is hostile
and not worth playing any more.
You the Character
Each Player Character you create is controlled by
you. It is up to you what sort of Character you are,
what skills you buy and how you want to play the
Character.
Chapter 2: Basics of Gameplay
Basics of Live Action role-playing
Player Characters
Player Characters, also known as PCs, are those
which have been created by Players for them to play.
They gain Experience Points by attending events,
these Experience Points are automatically converted
into Skill Points which are then used to purchase
Skills for their Character. A Player has full control of
their Character and is free to do what they want
within the limits of the rules of the game. They can be
a brave protector of the weak, a cowardly thief, or
even just brood in the shadows.
All Player Characters are required to carry the tags
for all items their character is. They must also carry
their Character's Skill Card. These documents are
necessary in the event there is any confusion or
question about your Character's abilities. If you have
reason to believe that another Player is using abilities
their Character shouldn't have you are permitted to
ask to view the documents for the item or the
Character's Skill Card. Asking to see another Player's
documentation must always be done in a courteous
manner. Alternatively, you may mention your
concerns to a member of Staff who will investigate
the matter.
Non-Player Characters
Non-Player Characters, also known as NPCs, are
those which have been created by the Plot Marshals
who run the game. They are created to progress a
story or narrative and range from goblins, to mindless
undead, to powerful extraplanar creatures, to high
ranking nobility. While they are not Player Characters
they should not be treated differently or somehow
less real. The only difference is that NPCs are under
the control of Plot Marshals, rather than the people
playing them.
As with Player Character, Non-Player Characters
are required to carry documentation which lists the
Character's Skills and abilities. This documentation
may be carried by a single member of the group if
they are playing identical creatures. Alternatively, the
abilities of all monsters may be affirmed by the
encounter's Marshal. This is not unusual in adventure
modules which are under the direct observation of a
member of the Chapter's Plot. Do not argue with an
encounter's Marshal during the adventure. If you
believe that something is wrong or was mishandled,
report it to another member of staff. Please note that
some creatures are extremely powerful and may in
fact have abilities that seem to be extraordinary or
unfair but if you have concerns, you are always free
to report them to event Staff during, or after the event.
Being In Game
This is the normal state for all participants. It
means that you are playing the game and can be
interacted with by other Characters. Player
Characters remain In Game throughout the entire
Event unless told differently by Marshals. This means
you eat, drink and sleep in game. This also means
that everything you say is said by your Character and
so you should do your best to make sure what you
say is appropriate to the situation. There is no rule
that allows you to touch a fist to your head to talk out
of game or anything similar.
Being Out of Game
Many Players who are playing as NPCs for the
Event frequently need to go Out of Game while
travelling to or from adventure sites or before making
their entrance to interact with the players. All Out of
Game Players wear white headbands. A Player who
is Out of Game does not exist and cannot be
interacted with in game. Unless directly engaged by a
Player who is Out of Game, Characters who are In
Game should do their best to ignore everything done
or said by an Out of Game Player. In the event that a
Player Character needs to go out of game, for
medical reasons or to get up in the middle of the night
to go to the bathroom and already removed their
costume, they may do so Out of Game. In all other
cases Players need to obtain permission from the
game Marshals to do so.
Game Items
Crucible uses a variety of Physical
Representations to represent items in the game.
Items generally fall into two categories; tagged or
untagged. Mundane items, such as a common steel
sword or suit of armour, which lack any sort of special
ability beyond being that item do not have tags.
These items may be broken or destroyed while on an
adventure but otherwise do not need to be tracked.
Potions, Scrolls, and similar items store game effects
and have tags authored by the Character who
created them and expire at the end of the Event.
Items which have special powers such as magical
weapons or are just valuable items have tags which
describe their special properties as well as their
expiration date. These last items may exist for
several months, or even years. As these items are
part of the game, they can be interacted with, stolen
or sold. Coin can be considered an additional type of
game item but it has no tag and serves only as
currency.
Out of Game Items
While items which have direct interaction with
game mechanics are considered game items, others
are not. A Character's personal items, such as;
footwear, clothing and props, are normally not part of
the game and should be respected. As these items
are never tagged, they cannot be stolen from the
Character. In the event that these items are
accidentally stolen they must be turned over
immediately to Staff so they can be returned to their
owner.
Out of Game Areas
Certain areas, such as bathrooms and under beds,
are designated Out of Game areas. These areas can
never be used to hide in game items. Any game items
which are stored in these areas must be placed there
before the beginning of the Event and cannot be
removed until the end of the Event. No Player is
permitted to search through another Player's property
when it is stored in such an area. Additionally, combat
can never take place inside of such an area. Finally,
these areas cannot be used to escape from danger.
Hours of Play
Crucible is a twenty-four hour game. Once the
game begins it continues until the end of the Event.
This means that you eat and sleep in game and can
even be attacked during these times.
Combat
Crucible is a light touch combat system. This
means that people strike each other with padded
weapons and packets of seed in order to represent
deadly attacks from weapons or magic spells. There
is no minimum amount of force needed for a hit to
count and you should only hit hard enough for your
opponent to feel the hit. If you believe you are being
hit too hard, mention it to your opponent. Also if you
are sure you've landed several hits on your opponent
you are free to mention it. Some Players wear large
costumes or thick armour and they may not feel your
hits under all of that padding.
Role-Playing
Crucible is a role-playing game. This means that all
Players are playing the part of their Character in a
consistent world all as part of cooperative storytelling.
The Event's Plot Marshals provide the basic story
and it is up to the Player Characters to engage with
and help that story unfold. All Players should do their
best to keep conversations in game avoiding
references to the real world. We all love pop culture
references, but try to cut back or disguise them in a
more in game way. Perhaps a new television series is
instead referred to as a book series or a new
blockbuster film is instead referred to as a play. In
either case, please keep such discussions to a
minimum during play.
The Flow of Play
Registration and Inspection
All Players who intend on attending the game,
either as NPCs or PCs, must Register their arrival.
This is done in order to track both their attendance
and their presence on the site. Additionally, all
Physical Representations for Armour and Weapons
must be inspected at the start of each Event to
ensure that they are safe for use.
Opening Ceremonies
Before the game begins, all Players must attend
opening ceremonies. This is where site rules and
important game information are disclosed as well.
This includes the identities of the Marshal teams, first
aiders and other important safety information.
Game On
Once opening ceremonies have concluded, the
game begins. All Characters begin the game at full
Hit Points, all of their Skills and whatever protective
effects, such as Magic Shields, they are able to cast
using their own Skills. Those who came into play as
part of a group have access to all protective effects
which can be cast by one or more of the group's
members.
Holds
This is a temporary suspension of play in response
to a safety concern or to describe an effect or scene
which cannot be effectively communicated during
normal play. During a Hold, the game is paused,
Skills cannot be used and everyone should remain
where they were at the time the Hold was called
unless instructed to do otherwise. Once a Hold has
no reason to continue a Marshal will perform a
three(3) second countdown and game play will
resume.
Reset
At 06:00 AM all Skills are Reset. All Characters
regain the use of all Skills. Effects which state that
they end at Reset immediately end. All Effects which
have a duration of Charged, Concentration or
Sustained immediately end. Characters who are on
an Adventure during Reset are not affected until the
Adventure ends unless told otherwise by the Marshal.
Abilities which Reset at the end of the Day or are
usable once per Day do not Reset at 06:00 AM. The
time between Resets is referred to as a "Reset
Period".
End of Day
At 06:00 PM all Skills Reset. All Characters regain
the use of all Skills. Effects which state that they end
at Reset or Day immediately end. All Effects which
have a duration of Charged, Concentration or
Sustained immediately end. Characters who are on
an Adventure during Reset are not affected until the
Adventure ends unless told otherwise by the Marshal.
The time between Resets is referred to as a "Reset
Period".
Game Off
At the end of the Event game play ends. Effects
which state that they end at Day, Event, or Reset
immediately end. All Effects which have a duration of
Charged, Concentration or Sustained immediately
end. All Items created by Players which expire at the
end of the Event, such as Potions and Scrolls, expire.
Creating a Character
Step 1) Decide on who your Character is.
Do you want to play a cunning rogue? An elven
archer? A brutal Orkish warrior? Having a clear vision
for what you want your Character to be is an
important first step and it guides many of your other
decisions as your Character goes from initial inkling
to fully realised hero.
Step 2) Choose a People and Heritage.
Crucible has thirty(30) different Heritages to
choose from. These vary from the simple common
Cosmopolitan Human, to the staples of fantasy
including; High Elves, Dwarves, Orks and Halflings,
to the more unusual in the Falgoth, Verdant and
Dul'Sidhe. A Character's Heritage determines any
makeup and costuming requirements as well as their
Heritage Traits which determines the cost of any
Heritage Skills the Character will later purchase.
Step 3) Choose your Skill Priorities.
Crucible does not use a class system for its
Characters. Instead Players choose Skill Priorities.
There are four Skill Groups; Crafting, Expertise,
Fighting and Magic while Priorities range from A
through to D. The cost of all Skills in the Priority A
Skill Group is the lowest while Priority B is slightly
higher, with Priority C being higher, and the skills in
the Priority D Skill Group are the highest cost. If the
Character has a Heritage Drawback it affects which
Priorities can be assigned to which Skill Groups.
Regardless of how you allocate Skill priorities, all
Characters start with ten(10) Hit Points and the ability
to wear twenty(20) Armour Points. Both of these
values can be increased by Skills such as Toughness
and Wear Extra Armour respectively.
Step 4) Spend Skill Points.
All Characters start with sixty(60) Skill Points which
they can use to purchase any Skills they can afford
and for which they meet any prerequisites. It's likely
that you won't be able to afford every Skill you want
to purchase so it's a good idea to figure out what you
want to build towards and then work backwards to
figure out which Skills you need to buy first.
Step 5) Create your Character's Costuming.
Costuming is a big part of bringing your Character
to life. Many people begin playing with little more than
a pair of sweatpants, a plain tabard and a borrowed
weapon. It's perfectly acceptable not to have the best
outfit, though many people compete for this title.
What's important is that your costume is comfortable
and safe to wear. Avoid sharp protrusions of any kind
as these can damage weapons, rendering them
unsafe.
While there is generally no wrong way to dress as
your Character there are several limitations in place
to help maintain the in game atmosphere. Avoid
denim clothing, especially blue denim like jeans.
Printed shirts are also not appropriate. Minimise
zippers as much as possible. In general, costumes
should look appropriate for a medieval fantasy
setting. Electronic devices should also be kept hidden
as little spoils a dramatic moment more than
someone playing games on their phone.
Chapter 3: Peoples and Their Heritages
Peoples of the World
The world is made up of many different types of
Peoples. Peoples is the collective term for all the
world's groups of intelligent creatures able to form a
recognizable society.. This means that while certain
groups of creatures, such as giant ants, may have
societies, they are not considered people as their
society is not one which could be considered
civilised.
The following is a collection of currently approved
Peoples available to Player Characters. All such
Peoples are considered to be the Creature Type:
Humanoid.
Costuming Requirements
Each Heritage has certain requirements for what
must be included in their costume. All members of a
Heritage are required to meet all such requirements.
Characters may not, unless it is given in their
Costume Suggestions, include any make-up or
prosthetic additions to their costuming outside of what
is prescribed in this section.
Costuming Suggestions
In order to help people with their costuming ideas,
and to introduce additional customisation options for
a Heritage's physical traits, this section includes
suggestions of things to include in your Character's
costuming. All suggestions made within this section
are optional at adherence to them are at the Player's
sole discretion.
Heritages
Though there are distinctly recognizable groups of
Peoples, each group is further split into heritages.
Heritages may be distinct from each other due to
physical characteristics, or by special abilities, or
simply by significant cultural differences.
Special Abilities
All Heritage Groups, or Peoples, such as Elves,
share a Special Ability. This ability is automatically
available to all Heritages in its corresponding group.
This ability functions even if the Character is under an
effect which prevents the use of Skills such as Drain.
Heritage Abilities
These abilities are automatically available to all
members of a particular Heritage. As with their
People Group's Special Ability, these abilities are
usable even if the Character is under an effect which
would prevent them from using Skills such as Drain.
The only exception to this is Heritage Abilities which
grant Proficiency with Weapons or Claws. A
Character under such an effect is unable to attack or
defend as though they bought with Skill Points.
Heritage Traits
Each Character is able to choose at least two(2)
Heritage Traits from a list given under each Heritage's
description. Some Heritages have access to the
Versatile Trait which, if chosen as one of the
Character's Traits, allows the Character to choose a
single Trait from a list of additional Traits. A Character
may choose to purchase a third Heritage Trait. If they
do so they must also choose a Heritage Drawback.
Heritage Drawbacks
A Character who has chosen three(3) Heritage
Traits must also choose a Heritage Drawback. These
place limits or special restrictions on a Character's
choices for Skill Priority. There is no way to overcome
a Heritage Drawback.
The Elves
The Elven people are known for their long lives,
pointed ears, ancient cultures, and exceptional
devotion to the arts.
Special Ability
Iron Will
Once per Reset, Elves can negate a single
Compulsion effect, by which they would be affected,
as per the Skill Resist Compulsion. Additionally, once
per Reset, an Elf may Rest in order to recharge this
ability allowing them to benefit from its effects a
maximum of twice per Reset. The call for this ability is
"Resist".
Deep Elf [Elf]
The Deep Elves can be found both far below the
surface in ancient cities and in the deepest reaches
of ancient magical forests where the dense canopy of
trees blots out the sun. They thrive in darkness with
many being greatly averse to sunlight. A rigid caste
system and societal pressures to adhere to it ensure
their people's continued survival in their often
inhospitable lands.
Costuming Requirements: All Deep elves have dark
grey or purple skin and pointed ears.
Costuming Suggestions: Many have white hair, but
some, especially those who have interbred with other
peoples, have more common hair colours.
Abilities:
Double Throw: Whenever a Deep Elf delivers an
attack using a Thrown Weapon they may throw
one(1) additional Thrown Weapon. Any creature
struck by either Weapon is affected once by the
attack. This additional weapon, and any others the
Character may throw, must be thrown with the same
hand and must be thrown at the same time.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Fast
Stealthy
Willful
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Lower Caste
(Magic and Fighting cannot be Priority A or B)
Expertise Devotion
(Expertise must be Priority A)
High Elf [Elf]
According to them, they are the most ancient and
greatest heritage to live in this world. While it is true
that they have the greatest cities and benefit from a
history which has seen the rise and fall of civilizations
and even predates the arrival of many of the other
peoples there is some debate as to whether or not
their claims are completely true. High Elves tend to
be haughty and aloof, often self superior and
overconfident in their abilities. Though their culture
has earned this position it is not unusual for them to
group the achievements of their people with their
own, leading many to finding them entitled and
insufferable.
Costuming Requirements: Pointed ears. At least
three pieces of conspicuous metallic jewellery,
usually gold or silver, in the form of earrings, tiaras,
rings, bracelets, necklaces and similar accessories.
Costuming Suggestions: High Elves are typically
well groomed and dress formally.

Heritage Abilities:
Invested Weapon: A High Elf may during any
Rest, attune to a single Weapon in their possession.
The Weapon acts as though it had a Magic Aura
Ritual effect. The Invested Weapon allows the
Character to deliver a Killing Blow that counts as
Magic. The Weapon has this ability only when
wielded by the Character who Invested it. Though this
ability is usable at will, the Character can have no
more than one Invested Weapon at a time.

Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Magical
Martial
Willful
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Pacifist
(Fighting cannot be above Priority C)
Traditionalist
(Cannot purchase Heritage Skills belonging to Traits
they do not possess)
Rift Elf [Elf]
Claiming to come from worlds beyond our own, the
Rift Elves are a mysterious group that just showed up
without warning. They possess powerful control over
elemental energy and magical forces.
Costuming Requirements: Like all elves, the Rift
Elves have long Pointed ears. They have a single
rune or gem set somewhere upon their forehead.
Costuming Suggestions: Rift Elves tend to favour
stars, moons and other celestial objects in their
clothing and accessories.
Heritage Abilities:
Elemental Power: Once per Reset, a Rift Elf may
attune themselves to any of the following Damage
Types; Chaos, Destruction, Dream, Essence, Fire,
Ice, Light, Lightning, Order, Reason, Shadow, or
Stone. The Character may Invoke this ability to use
the chosen Damage Type, for all of their Weapon
Damage Calls, against a single opponent at will until
the end of Combat.
Additionally, while using the above ability, the
Character may replace the Damage Type of any
spells, which normally allows the Character to choose
from the following list; Fire, Ice, Lightning, Stone, with
the chosen Damage Type against the target of this
ability.
Alternatively, they may expend this ability to negate
a single effect causing any of the above Damage
Types as per the Ward ability. When used in this form
the Call is "Ward".
A Character may, once per Reset, recover the use
of this ability by Resting allowing it to be used, at
most, twice per Reset.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Fast
Magical
Spiritual
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Academic
(Magic must be higher Priority than Fighting)
Trickster
(Magic and Expertise must be consecutive Priorities)
Tranquil Elf [Elf]
Another heritage of elves, nearly as ancient as the
High Elves, the Tranquil are a heritage who believe
that enlightenment can be obtained only by mastery
of the self. To this end they have detached
themselves emotionally from the world. Most show
little to no emotional reaction and some even feel
pain when doing so. They seldom use force
preferring gentle discourse but when no other option
is available, they can carry out acts of violence with a
cold detachment that is both unnerving and
devastatingly efficient.
Costuming Requirements: Like all elves, the
Tranquil have long Pointed ears. They have skin
which is completely white, though some decorate
their lips or eyes with colour to indicate association,
or position.
Costuming Suggestions: Some Tranquil have
metallic coloured skin appearing to have a slightly
silverish, brazen or even coppery appearance to their
flesh.
Heritage Abilities:
Telepathy: The Character may enter the mind of a
willing target allowing the Character to view the
target's memories and heal any traumatic memories
allowing them to remove most forms of role-playing
insanity. The process of healing takes at least five(5)
minutes.
Alternatively, the Character may expend this ability
allowing them to deliver a Magic Inspiration to
themselves or another, by Touch. If this ability is
expended in this way it cannot be used, for either
effect, until the next Reset period.
Tranquillity: The Character is Resistant to the
effects; Berserk and Fear. This means that they
reduce the duration of any Berserk or Fear effect to
ten(10) seconds. Effects which are delivered as
Piercing or Arcane bypass this ability.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Magical
Psychic
Willful
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Academic
(Magic must be higher Priority than Fighting)
Crafting Devotion
(Crafting must be Priority A)
Wild Elf [Elf]
Wild Elves can be found in forests, deserts and
pretty much anywhere else where they can be away
from civilization. This is not due to them being
somehow less "civilized" than others, they have
simply chosen to live alongside nature. As a result,
they tend to be very minimal when it comes to their
possessions, able to live without the comforts most
people consider essential. They have a strong
respect for nature and the rules which govern it.
Costuming Requirements: Like all other Elves, the
Wild Elves have pointed ears. They decorate
themselves with furs and facial markings of one or
more colours. These markings may be of any colour
though green, brown and red are most common.
Regardless of the form the markings take, no single
colour may cover more than 25% of the face and the
total makeup can cover no more than 50% of the
face. This is to avoid appearing as another Heritage.
Costuming Suggestions: Wild Elves tend to dress
in clothing meant to camouflage themselves with their
surroundings. Some also wear furs.
Heritage Abilities:
Forest's Mark: The Wild Elf may Invoke this ability
on a single Character designating it the target for this
ability. Once their Mark has been chosen, the
Character increases all Weapon Damage against
their target by two(2). This ability lasts until the end of
combat. Alternatively, the Character may expend this
effect early by delivering a single Alchemy, Martial Art,
or Spell from their memory as Piercing. Once per
Reset, the Character may Rest to regain the use of
this ability. The call for this ability is "I invoke Forest's
Mark against you.".
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Fast
Wild
Willful
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Hunter
(Expertise must be higher Priority than Crafting)
Limited Magic
(Magic Cannot be above Priority C)
Faekin People
The Faekin are a group of peoples who are
supposedly descended from Fae who became
trapped on this world after being tricked or being
banished. Whether this is true or not matters little as
there is no denial that the magical Faekin are, at the
very least, very similar to the true Fae.
Special Ability
Eldritch Bloodline
Free Skill: Magical Aptitude
Dul'Sidhe [Faekin]
The Dul'Sidhe are a group of Faekin known for
being tied to death. Supposedly descended from the
Dullahan and other thematically morbid Fae.
Whatever their true ancestry, the Dul'Sidhe are
known to be experts in all areas of death from
causing it, to preventing it and even speaking to
those who have moved on.
Dul'Sidhe adventurers tend to be on the lookout for
what their people call "a beautiful death". What this
entails varies between individuals but it typically
involves a death which is welcomed as a friend, or is
the result of willing self sacrifice to save the innocent.
Costuming Requirements: Collar worn around
throat. Black around eyes.
Costuming Suggestions: Many Dul'Sidhe dress in
funerary garb or monochromatic clothing.
Heritage Abilities:
Dream of Death: The first time this Character
enters their Death Count they immediately receive
the benefits of a Contingency Arcane Life effect. This
ability is usable once per Reset. This effect cannot be
suppressed or prevented from triggering.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Martial
Spiritual
Stealthy

Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Limited Magic
(Magic Cannot be above Priority C)
Hunter
(Expertise must be higher Priority than Crafting)
Faun [Faekin]
The Faun are supposedly the descendants of
Satyrs and nature Fae. They value personal
freedoms and dislike any form of oppressive rule as
well as magical compulsions which rob a person of
their senses. Their incredible life spans lead them to
seek new experiences and the oldest often choose to
forget the past so they aren't bored.
As adventurers the Faun tend to be there for the
experience and a desire to help others gain new
experiences. They offer advice to anyone who'll
listen, though this advice may not always make sense
as some will deceive just to see what happens.
Costuming Requirements: Antlers or small horns.
Costuming Suggestions: Some Faun have large
ears, either pointed or deer-like, and some have
hoofed feet but these are not requirements.
Heritage Abilities:
Free Will: A Faun may, at will, spend sixty(60)
seconds role-playing with another creature. If they
choose to do so, they may deliver, by Touch, an
"Arcane Dispel Compulsion" effect against the target
of this ability. If used, this ability must be used against
the target of their role-play
Once per Reset, the Character may, instead of the
Dispel Compulsion effect, deliver an Arcane Remove
Enthral effect. If the Character has the Break
Compulsion Skill as well, the Character may use the
Break Charm ability an unlimited number of times
each Reset.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Magical
Trader
Willful
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
No Heroes
(Fighting and Expertise Cannot be Priority A)
Spellcraft
(Magic and Crafting must be consecutive Priorities)
Gnome [Faekin]
Gnomes are optimistic inventors with big ideas and
just need the right tools to bring them to life. They
often move from one project to another and always
chase after perfection. They are easily distracted by
their next big idea and are curious about how things
work.
Gnome adventurers are often out to find the new
and unusual. They love puzzles and many things
which challenge their minds including figuring out the
answer to the one question gnomes ask themselves
the most; "How am I gonna get out of this one?"
Costuming Requirements: Large rounded ears or
bulbous nose.
Costuming Suggestions: Most Gnomes carry a
variety of tools, puzzles and other random knick-
knacks they collect or find interesting. A common
fashion choice is a tall conical hat.
Heritage Abilities:
Frequent Ideas: May benefit from the Inspiration
effect twice per Reset.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Magical
Skilled
Trader
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Crafting Devotion
(Crafting must be Priority A)
Nimble Fingers
(Crafting and Expertise must be Consecutive
Priorities)
Sprytling [Faekin]
Among the Faekin, none are more magical than
the Sprytling. These beings are imbued with a love
for magic and life that borders on obsession. They
love food, music, drinks, people, art and are just
happy to be around. As a result, they are often
viewed as exhausting by other peoples who don't
understand the Sprytling's genuine enthusiasm for
the world around them.
When a Sprytling decides to be an
adventurer it is almost always done to meet new
people. Every new person is a potential friend and
every enemy is also a potential friend. This does not
mean they are naive, Sprytlings are fully aware of the
danger posed by some creatures. They just have the
optimism and positivity to hope for the best.

Costuming Requirements: Sparkles and wings.
Costuming Suggestions: Being creatures of a
capricious nature, Sprytlings tend to dress in a very
colourful manner. Many carry objects related to their
most recent fixation.
Heritage Abilities:
Faerie Dust: When a Sprytling first enters their
Dying Count, or if they enter their Death Count as the
result of a Death effect, they may deliver a special
packet attack causing one(1) point of Magic Force
damage to any target struck by one or more packets.
Though the Character may throw any number of
packets, all packets must be thrown at the same time
and must be thrown within ten(10) seconds of this
ability being triggered. Once this ability has been
used, the Character must spend Rest before they can
use it again. The call for this ability is "Death Throes:
1 Magic Force"
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Fast
Magical
Wild
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Pacifist
(Fighting cannot be above Priority C)
Trickster
(Magic and Expertise must be Consecutive Priorities)
Tuathan [Faekin]
Standing in contrast to the other Faekin, the
Tuathan are very serious. They are great sages and
warriors with each working to be the best in their
particular field. Almost every Tuathan has something
they have mastered to nearly, and sometimes literally,
a supernatural extent.
Tuathan adventurers always have something to
prove. it's usually that they are number one at
whatever it is they do. Those who aren't the best will
at least try their best and do whatever it takes to
improve and leave their peers behind. That being
said, they recognize that while they might be good at
one thing, they aren't the best at everything and
cooperation is sometimes the only way to succeed.
Costuming Requirements: Third eye on forehead.
Glittery or ornately detailed makeup.
Costuming Suggestions: The Tuathan tend to
extensively favour the colours blue, green, and yellow
in their clothing. Most often in the form of silks and
shiny clothing.
Heritage Abilities:
Fragment of Light: Once per Reset, a Tuathan
may deliver a single effect, which is normally
delivered as Physical(-Strike), as Magic(-Strike)
instead of its normal Delivery Type. If this effect
causes Weapon Type damage, the Damage Type
changes to "Force". For example, a Tuathan can
deliver an attack causing "50 Physical-Strike Iron"
damage as "50 Magic Force" or "50 Magic-Strike
Force". This ability can be recharged by the Tuathan
spending five(5) minutes Resting.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Fast
Magical
Spiritual
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Pacifist
(Fighting cannot be above Priority C)
Magical Devotion
(Magic must be Priority A)
The Goblinoids
Goblinoids are a group of humanoid who were
previously mistaken as monsters but have now
become a regular part of society. They are hardy and
capable of surviving in the most inhospitable regions
of the world. This led to many of them following
nomadic lifestyles until eventually stumbling onto the
"soft lands" populated by the non-goblinoid peoples.
Many of these early encounters had the foreign
goblinoids viewed as a brutal invading force which
was reciprocated by both groups Eventually, small
peaceful groups were encountered and the
embarrassing War of Ignorance was brought to an
end.
Special Ability
Peerless Survivors
The first time a Goblinoid enters their Dying count
they automatically gain the effects of a Contingency
Physical Stabilise. Once triggered, the Goblinoid
must spend five(5) minutes Resting before they can
use it again.
Goblin [Goblinoid]
The first Goblinoids encountered by small groups
of humans, the goblins served as accidental
ambassadors of first contact. Unfortunately their
curiosity and enthusiasm was mistaken as attempts
at thievery, sabotage and caused no end to problems.
Goblins are a very curious, somewhat reckless
group of people who are inventive and clever, but
frequently make mistakes or fail to plan
ahead.Though this is true in general, there are still
many goblins who are careful planners, cunning
warriors and even well trained mages.
Costuming Requirements: Green skin. Pointed
green ears.
Costuming Suggestions: Goblins tend to favour
black and dark colours. This is done in order to hide
stains, powder burns and blood making them seem
more careful than they really are.
Heritage Abilities:
Tamper: A Goblin can spend a sixty(60) count
tampering with an item in order to deliver a "Physical
Break <Item>" against the item by Touch.
Alternatively, this ability can be expended in
order to deliver a "Physical-Strike Break <Item>"
effect. Once expended for the latter effect, the
Character must Rest in order to regain the use of this
ability.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Fast
Skilled
Survivor
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Crafting Devotion
(Crafting must be Priority A)
Limited Magic
(Magic Cannot be above Priority C)
Kobolds [Goblinoid]
Kobolds are a group of subterranean dwelling
creatures who make their homes in the most
dangerous areas, near the most dangerous creatures
that can be found. As a result, they have no time for
many of the cultural hang ups of other peoples being
completely egalitarian in society treating anyone as
an equal so long as they are willing to help stave off
death for another day.
Costuming Requirements: Reptilian or canine
snout. Steampunk goggles.
Costuming Suggestions: Kobolds tend to avoid
anything that is not practical in their outfits. Tools,
aprons, knives are all good but capes and frills are
dangerous.
Heritage Abilities:
Light Step: Kobolds have Trap Resistance,
reducing the damage from Traps by half, rounded
down, and reducing the duration of non-damaging
Traps to ten(10) seconds. The call for this ability is
"Reduced".
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Fast
Survivor
Trader
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Feeble Frame
(Fighting Cannot be higher Priority than Expertise)
Nimble Fingers
(Crafting and Expertise must be Consecutive
Priorities)
Ogri [Goblinoid]
The Ogri are, unlike the other Goblinoids, patient
and ponderous with great skill at magic. It was they
who first negotiated to gain their people's recognition
as intelligent civilised creatures rather than mindless
brutes.
Ogri are all capable of using some magic, though
this is typically limited. Those who choose to focus on
magic excel at it. They often favour defensive magic
but some are still capable warriors.
Costuming Requirements: Light green to blue skin.
Lower Tusks or Prominent Brow
Costuming Suggestions: The Ogri tend to favour
fine ornamental clothing. Robes, hoods, sashes, and
grand capes are not out of place.
Heritage Abilities:
Natural Magician: At the start of each Day, the
6:00 PM Reset, choose a single School of Magic.
Once per Reset, the Character is able to cast a single
Spell, below 6th level, from the chosen School. If the
Character has the Natural Magic Skill, they may cast
a second spell, below 6th Level, from the chosen
School in place of this Skill's normal effect.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Magical
Psychic
Tough
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Pacifist
(Fighting cannot be above Priority C)
Crafting Devotion
(Crafting must be Priority A)
Ork [Goblinoid]
Orks are a group of proud and highly skilled
warriors valuing a complex code of honour. Though
each Ork has their own code, they all share basics of
not attacking an unarmed opponent, or intentionally
harming the helpless. Sadly this is where many Orks
draw the line leading to many brutal battles.
Orks are neither stupid not savage. They are
trained from an early age as hunters and warriors.
Though magic is often seen as secondary, they still
boast a fair number of capable mages.
Costuming Requirements: Green skin. Blackened
Eyes.
Costuming Suggestions: Orks tend to favour
function over form. As a result their clothing and
armour tends to look tossed together or only half
finished.
Heritage Abilities:
Blooded Weapon: Once per Reset, upon
delivering a successful Killing Blow the Character's
Weapon Damage is increased by one(1) until the end
of Combat. The Ork may Rest in order to regain the
use of this ability.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Martial
Strong
Wild
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Limited Magic
(Magic Cannot be above Priority C)
Fighting Devotion
(Fighting must be Priority A)
Troll [Goblinoid]
Trolls are large, durable, and nearly unkillable.
They excel and enjoy combat to the extent that many
others see them brutes. This is not true however as
many trolls have been shown to be capable of
crafting,stealth and even the occasional mage has
been seen.
Trolls tend to live in the moment, often doing things
solely for the sake of entertainment. Boredom to a
troll is intolerable and many have started a fight just
because they haven't done anything in a while.
Costuming Requirements: Green to yellow-green
skin. Long nose or short horns.
Costuming Suggestions: Trolls don't really have
much of a fashion sense. As a result they often grab
whatever is closest or most easily obtainable making
them look even more chaotically coutured than even
Sprytlings.
Heritage Abilities:
Heal Good: Whenever a Troll is affected by
Healing which would put them above their maximum
Hit Point total, they gain a number of Fortification
Points equal to the amount of over healing. The
maximum number of Fortification Points the
Character can gain from this ability is ten(10).
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Strong
Survivor
Tough
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Limited Dexterity
(Crafting and Expertise Cannot be above Priority C)
Martial Focus
(Fighting must be higher Priority than Magic)

The Humans
Humans are the most populous and widespread of
the peoples. They also have a tendency of slapping
labels on things even if those labels turn out to be
wrong.
There have been many people who have said that
humans are this way due to their short lives and are
stubborn because they don't have the lifespan to go
back and do it right the second time. As a result,
humans are always moving forward, even if they
know what's in store for them is terrible they will keep
going, move past it and continue on.
Special Ability
Creativity
Once per Reset, a Human may deliver a Physical
Inspiration effect by Touch.
Barbarian [Human]
These humans live in the wild regions of the world
in small nomadic societies. They waste little and are
careful in their hunting and practices making sure that
their tribe is fed, safe and healthy.
Many Barbarians associated themselves with a
particular totem. A totem is an abstraction of natural
forces which they believe are embodied by a magical
animal. It is possible for two different tribes to both
have the same ideals and come to the conclusion
that a different animal represents those ideas.
Barbarians are able to call upon the power of their
totem to grant themselves strength of body and mind,
often gaining visions or insight into their problems.
Unfortunately this was initially mistaken as
superstition as it was a form of magic that was not
known to those who observed it leading to the group
being branded as Barbarians.
Costuming Requirements: All Barbarians wear fur,
this can be belts, cloaks, mantles, or any other fur
addition to their costume.
Costuming Suggestions: Barbarians tend to include
simple iron jewellery or wooden trinkets depicting
important ancestral, historical or mythical figures. It's
also not unusual for them to wear makeshift
bandoleers of useful items.
Heritage Abilities:
Rage: Once per Reset, a Barbarian may enter into
a Rage granting themselves the following effects;
Physical Vorpal 5 and Immunity to Berserk and Fear
effects so long as they have at least one(1) Charge of
the Vorpal effect granted by this ability. Once per
Reset, the Character may spend five(5) minutes
Resting in order to recharge this ability allowing them
to use, at most, two(2) times per Reset.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Spiritual
Strong
Wild
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Martial Focus
(Fighting must be higher priority than Magic)
Traditionalist
(Cannot purchase Heritage Skills belonging to Traits
they do not possess)
Cosmopolitan [Human]
These humans live their lives within, or in support
of a city. They may work on a farm that provides one
with food, or they may work in the sewers beneath
the roads of the capital. These humans know that the
best people, best food and best life can be found in
cities where all the people come together.
Despite this positive outlook, they tend to assume
that what they know is true. This leads to many of the
other peoples looking upon them with contempt or as
you would upon a child who is confidently incorrect.
Some of them are able to move past this and see that
the world is strange and full of wonders and the best
way forward is to keep learning.
Costuming Requirements: None
Costuming Suggestions: Cosmopolitan humans
have a tendency to wear tailored clothing but the
styles vary greatly between regions. Their clothing is
a mix of comfort and vanity.
Heritage Abilities:
Educated: The Player chooses a single
Background Profession Skill. The Character gains it
for free.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Trader
Versatile (Any One)
Versatile (Choose One: Spiritual, Skilled, Survivor)
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are
taken)
Limited Magic
(Magic Cannot be above Priority C)
Traditionalist
(Cannot purchase Heritage Skills belonging to Traits
they do not possess)
Debarian [Human]
The Debarians are a group of humans who revere
the dead and are able to speak to them. They were
initially thought of as charlatans and frauds but, over
time, proved that they were right. Despite this, many
still view them with suspicion since most can't see the
invisible beings to which they speak.
Debarians are extremely varied due to their
upbringing but the two things they agree on is fair
exchange and that blood is more valuable than gold.
Though there is a life beyond, it is only in this one
where we can discover the happiness and memories
that will keep us warm for eternity.
Costuming Requirements: Speaker Doll (Small doll
between ten(10) and thirty(30)cm in length) or
Speaker Mask.
Costuming Suggestions: Debarians wear clothing
meant to gather the attention of the Spirits with whom
they want to commune. As a result their style of dress
often includes bright primary colours although some
prefer pure white and others pure black.
Heritage Abilities:
Spirit Medium: All Debarians are able to see and
converse with Spirits. If the Character has the Seer
skill, their Rank is increased by one(1). Additionally,
they do not need to provide an offering for the
Beckon Haunting ability of the Seer Skill. The Seer
may choose whether their Beckon Haunting
summons a benevolent or malevolent Spirit.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Psychic
Spiritual
Versatile (Choose One: Skilled, Survivor, Trader)
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Pacifist
(Fighting cannot be above Priority C)
Spellcraft
(Magic and Crafting must be consecutive Priorities)
Dominion [Human]
Dominion humans value personal freedoms to an
extreme. Centuries ago they were enslaved by a
group of powerful mages. Though the people
eventually destroyed their old masters they were
fearful of the same thing happening again.
Dominion humans are cooperative with others and
believe in community and that the purpose of leaders
is to represent and speak for their people. They tend
to kill their leaders who speak and act for selfishly.
Costuming Requirements: Chain worn around the
neck or upon the shoulders.
Costuming Suggestions: Dominion humans, like
Cosmopolitan humans, tend to wear fitted clothing.
The Dominion however, tends to have a very military-
like style to their clothing with coats and high boots
being the norm.
Heritage Abilities:
Break Magic: Upon being affected by an effect
delivered as Magic(-Strike) the Character may
expend this Skill to reduce the duration to ten(10)
seconds. This Skill is usable at will, but the Character
must spend five(5) minutes Resting to recharge this
Skill. The call for this ability is "Reduced".
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Martial
Psychic
Versatile (Choose One: Skilled, Trader, Willful)
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Fighting Devotion
(Fighting must be Priority A)
Traditionalist
(Cannot purchase Heritage Skills belonging to Traits
they do not possess)
Halfling [Human]
There have been many rumours about how
Halflings got their name. Some believed they used to
live with giants, others believed it was because they
were the product of inter-breeding. Whatever the
reason, Halflings are a human heritage that value
entertainment, good food, good friends and living life
to the fullest.
Many Halflings venture into the world to seek
stories, exotic food and occasionally become an
accidental legend. As a result they tend to lean more
on being well rounded than extremely focused but
this is not always the case.
Costuming Requirements: Choose two; Hairy
brows, sideburns, hands, feet.
Costuming Suggestions: Some Halflings prefer to
go without shoes, but this style is more seen when
they are home or on vacation. Halflings tend to
maintain organised collections leading many to have
a large number of pouches and containers on their
person.
Heritage Abilities:
Charming Personality: Once per Reset, A Halfling
may spend sixty(60) seconds role-playing with
another creature. If they choose to do so, they may
deliver, by packet, an Arcane Charm against the
target of this ability. If used, this ability must be used
against the target of their role-play.
Alternatively, the Halfling may use this ability to
deliver a packet attack causing a "Physical Shun"
effect.
This ability can be used even if the Halfling is
under an effect which prevents the use of Skills. The
Character may, once per Reset, Rest in order to
regain the use of this ability.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Fast
Skilled
Stealthy
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Artisan
(Crafting Cannot be below Priority B)
No Heroes
(Fighting and Magic Cannot be Priority A)
The Shaped
The Shaped are reputed to be a group of peoples
who were artificially created by some mysterious and
long gone progenitor civilization called the Shapers.
Whether this is true or not the Heritages of the
Shapes all excel in particular areas of society.
The Shaped all have a strong sense of duty,
community and purpose with those who choose to
adventure being those who want to bring back
knowledge and materials which will benefit their
homes and families.
Special Ability
Gift of Purpose
All Shaped have the ability Drain Resistance. They
treat all Drain effects as Lesser reducing their
duration to ten(10) seconds. The call for this ability is
"Reduced". This ability does not apply to Drain effects
delivered as Piercing or Arcane.
Dwarf [Shaped]
Dwarves are bearded humanoids who are known
for their exceptional crafting abilities. Known for
producing both large scale engineering projects as
well as finely detailed works of art.
As a result, Dwarves are very cooperative with
their peers as they work together to accomplish their
shared goal. Unfortunately, Dwarves are also
reluctant to trust anyone who hasn't proven that they
are competent.
Dwarves honour their word and contracts as
without agreements on each party's commitments it
can lead to disappointment and mistakes.
Costuming Requirements: Costume Beard. If the
Player has a natural beard they may add extensions
or incorporate a number of trinkets, minimum
three(3), decorating or woven into the hair.
Costuming Suggestions: Dwarves are very proud
of their hair and often add decorations, or try bold
styles. They also prefer well made and well fitting
clothing including various items the Dwarf has made,
inherited, or been paid with.
Heritage Abilities:
Hammercraft: Mace Mastery.
If the Character is under an effect which prevents
the use of Skills, such as Drain, the Character is
unable to attack or defend with these Weapons.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Skilled
Tough
Trader
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Crafting Devotion
(Crafting must be Priority A)
Might and Magic
(Magic and Fighting must be consecutive Priorities)
Falgoth [Shaped]
The Falgoth are highly advanced slimes believed
to have been meant to act as a servant class by the
Shapers. Whatever their intentions, the nature of the
Falgoth are intelligent and capable, being able to
learn complex tasks in little time. Where they lack is
in innovations and philosophy. Falgoth imitate but
seldom create.
As adventurers, most Falgoth are out to collect
new skills for their pools, literal lakes of elder Falgoth
who have aggregated into a vast living library of
found knowledge.
Costuming Requirements: Shiny skin with blue tint.
Costuming Suggestions: Falgoth tend to avoid
restrictive clothing preferring loose fitting, or minimal
obstructions to their movements.
Heritage Abilities:
Malleable: The Character has Binding Resistance
reducing the duration of all Binding effects to ten(10)
seconds. Effects delivered as Piercing or Arcane
bypass this ability.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Magical
Stealthy
Survivor
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Limited Dexterity
(Crafting and Expertise Cannot be above Priority C)
Limited Magic
(Magic Cannot be above Priority C)

Narahk [Shaped]
The Narahk are a group of exceptionally skilled
warriors and peacekeepers. They value discipline,
law and order but also understand that mistakes can
happen and as long as you learn from them, you can
earn forgiveness and redemption.
Narahk often become adventurers as a way of
satisfying a primal urge to fight and protect others
with them often finding employment as mercenaries
so long as the battle is honourable.
Costuming Requirements: Large Horns and rune(s)
on face. Runes may also be found on limbs.
Costuming Suggestions: Narahk usually wear their
armour at all times. Even their spell casters still wear
armour sections. They tend to decorate their armour
with various runes which match those on their flesh.
Heritage Abilities:
Runic Flesh: Whenever a Narahk is healed by an
effect which would put them above their maximum Hit
Point total they may choose to have any remaining
healing restore a number of missing Armour Points
equal to the excess Healing, up to the Character's full
Armour Point Total. The Character may use this
ability to restore a number of Armour Points equal to
their maximum Wear Points before they must Rest in
order to use this ability again.
For example, If a Narahk, with a Wear Points total
of thirty(30), was missing five(5) Hit Points and
received twenty(20) points of Healing they would
recover their five(5) missing Hit Points, then the
remaining fifteen(15) would be applied towards their
Armour Point total. If the Character was wearing a
Physical Armour suit which was missing eight(8)
Armour Points it would recover the missing Armour
Points and the remaining seven(7) points of Healing
would be lost. The Character could still restore
twenty-two(22) Armour Point from this ability before
needing to Rest.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Martial
Strong
Tough
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Leadfoot
(Expertise must be lower Priority than Fighting)
Battlecraft
(Crafting and Fighting must be consecutive Priorities)
Skyvorne [Shaped]
The Skyvorne are composed of intellectuals, artists
and some of the finest thinkers the world has to offer.
Unfortunately their predilections towards academic
endeavours have left them somewhat lacking in
hands on work. As a result, they tend to separate
themselves from people who work for a living.
Those who can overcome this aversion towards
work become world travellers trying to unearth and
explain the secrets of the past as well as understand
the nature of the world. They are willing to work with
others to achieve these goals but have a tendency to
think their beliefs and theories are correct.
Costuming Requirements: Feathered eyebrows
and feathers in hair.
Costuming Suggestions: Skyvorne have a
tendency to wear fancy, layered clothing with intricate
details in bold colours with metallic accents. Their
accessories are equally ostentatious and
conspicuous.
Heritage Abilities:
Telepathy: The Character may enter the mind of a
willing target allowing the Character to view the
target's memories and heal any traumatic memories
allowing them to remove most forms of role-playing
insanity. The process of healing takes at least five(5)
minutes. Alternatively, the Character may expend this
ability allowing them to deliver a Magic Inspiration to
themselves or by Touch. If this ability is expended in
this way it cannot be used, for either effect, until the
next Reset period.
Feather Touch: Thrown Weapon Proficiency
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Magical
Psychic
Wild
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Academic
(Magic must be higher Priority than Fighting)
Traditionalist
(Cannot purchase Heritage Skills belonging to Traits
they do not possess)
Voxian [Shaped]
The Voxians are skilled diplomats, teachers and
communicators. They are often found as mediators
for complex social issues as well as on the stage
acting as thespians and musicians.
Voxians tend to get along with everyone being both
friendly and accommodating of others. This has led
them to being viewed, by some, with suspicion. Each
Voxian wears a mask which depicts characteristics of
another Heritage. In ages past these masks were
supposedly perfect imitations but in the modern world
they are little more than theatrical masks worn only to
give the artistic impression of the Voxian belonging to
another Heritage.
Costuming Requirements: Full face mask depicting
a Heritage.
Costuming Suggestions: Voxians tend to wear
bulky clothing which hides their body shape. Their
clothing is often imperfect, sleeves the wrong length,
seams not quite straight or other errors.
Heritage Abilities:
Commanding Voice: When using any ability which
allows them to throw a packet attack causing a
Compulsion effect, this Character may throw an
additional packet.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Psychic
Spiritual
Willful
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Trickster
(Magic and Expertise must be Consecutive Priorities)
Violence Aversion (Fighting and Expertise must be
below Priority B)
Zoan People
The Zoan Peoples are anthropomorphic creatures
of the natural world which are endowed with human-
like intelligence. While they vary widely in
appearance there are a small number of heritage
groups that can be recognized. The Zoan have a
number of origins, some are descendants of; animals
who were magically transformed into humanoids,
individuals who were punished by eldritch monsters,
lycanthropes and similar creatures or artificial beings
created in this form. Regardless of their origins, Zoan
are now counted among the Peoples of the world.
All Zoan have an intense sense of community
where all members of the group treat each other as
family. This does not mean that they always get
along, it just means they form deep bonds for the
sake of mutual survival.
Special Abilities
Pact Tactics
Any number of Zoan may form a group using a
special ceremony lasting a day and requiring five(5)
minutes of role-play. This role-play may be in the form
of discussion, a meal, singing competition or any
other group activity the members are comfortable
with. Once formed, the pack cannot be changed until
the next day. All members of the pack gain one of the
following benefits which must be agreed on by all
members;
Strength in Unity: All members of the pack gain
one(1) point of Strength. Increasing the damage done
by all weapon attacks by one(1). This effect does not
stack with any other effect which grants Strength.
Unified Defence: All members of the pack gain
five(5) additional Hit Points. This effect does not stack
with any other effect which grants the Hit Points other
than the Skills Toughness and Natural Toughness.
Hidden Numbers: All members of the pack gain
two(2) points of Cunning. Increasing the damage
done by all weapon attacks from behind their target
by two(2). This effect does not stack with any other
effect which grants Cunning.
Cooperative Hunt: All members of the pack gain
the ability to grant themselves a "Physical Ambush 1"
effect, or a "Physical Vorpal 1" effect. The Character
must Meditate in between uses of this ability.
Beastfolk [Zoan]
The Beastfolk are Zoan who appear to be part
mammal. They often display the behaviours of the
animal to which they resemble, but this is not always
the case. Some even deliberately avoid these
behaviours, viewing them as vulgar.
Beastfolk adventurers often operate as part of a
group where they are able to share information and
resources, increasing their chances of success in
whatever situation in which they find themselves.
Costuming Requirements: Mammalian animal
traits.
Costuming Suggestions: Beastfolk tend to favour
practical clothing foregoing fashion for the sake of
efficiency. This also means they have a tendency to
only replace clothing when it has been completely
destroyed.
Heritage Abilities:
Predator: The Character gains the ability to
Conjure a single Claw, up to a Long Sword in length,
and gains Proficiency in Claws. Additionally, the
Character may grant themselves a "Physical Ambush
1" effect. This effect can be recovered by Meditating.
If the Character has the Two Weapon Fighting
Skill, they may Conjure up to two(2) Claws at once in
order to wield one(1) in each hand.
If the Character is under an effect which prevents
the use of Skills, such as Drain, the Character is
unable to attack or defend with these Claws.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Versatile (Choose One; Strong, Fast, Tough)
Versatile (Choose One; Tough, Stealthy, Survivor)
Wild
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Claws Out
(Expertise or Fighting must be Priority A)
Limited Magic
(Magic Cannot be above Priority C)

Piscine [Zoan]
The Piscine possess varied but obvious aquatic
animal traits. Some appear fish-like while others have
an appearance that more closely resembles
mammals or reptiles. Supposedly there are even
cephalopod Piscine but these are extremely rare.
When they venture out to become adventurers
they often join up with other groups. They work
tirelessly and efficiently doing their best to help those
around them. Despite often being mistaken as
helpless, the Piscine boast a number of extremely
capable warriors and powerful mages.
Costuming Requirements: Aquatic animal traits.
Costuming Suggestions: Being aquatic, the Piscine
have a habit of dressing for the water, even when on
dry land. Inexplicably, many forego this clothing
during the rain or when actually on the water.
Heritage Abilities:
Keep Swimming: The Character has Sleep
Resistance. This ability reduces the duration of all
Sleep effects, other than those delivered as Arcane(-
Strike) or Piercing to ten(10) seconds. Additionally,
while under the Sleep effect, this creature, though still
unaware of its surroundings, may continue to move at
a normal walking pace.
Additionally, when the Character becomes
Unconscious, but not Dead, as a result of Hit Point
Damage, they may continue to move at a normal
walking rate for ten(10) seconds before having to fall
to the ground, as soon as it is safe to do so. The
Character cannot use Skills, or items, while in this
state.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Versatile (Choose One; Strong, Fast, Tough)
Versatile (Choose One; Magical, Psychic, Survivor)
Wild
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Stalker
(Expertise and Fighting must be consecutive
Priorities)
Limited Dexterity
(Crafting and Expertise Cannot be above Priority C)
Reptoid [Zoan]
Many Reptoids are offended by being in the same
group as the other Zoans but then again, Reptoids
tend to get mad at a lot of things. They are much
more solitary than the other Zoan forming groups
when they need to solve a large or complex problem
that they can't fix on their own.
Reptoid adventurers are often in heavy combat
roles where they are able to more easily demonstrate
their contributions to battle. This does not mean they
are always on the front lines, some are capable
casters, but they want their presence to be known
and recognized.
Costuming Requirements: Reptile or avian traits.
Costuming Suggestions: Reptoids tend to favour
scaled leather and metal armour. Even their regular
clothes have a tendency to follow this scaled theme.
Some Reptoids instead choose to favour a single
colour wearing only clothing of their preferred hue.
Heritage Abilities:
Venomous: The Character gains the ability to
Conjure a single Claw, up to a Long Sword in length,
and gains Proficiency in Claws. Additionally, the
Character may deliver, by Touch, a "Physical Vorpal
1" effect. This effect can be recovered by Meditating.
If the Character has the Two Weapon Fighting
Skill, they may Conjure up to two(2) Claws at once in
order to wield one(1) in each hand.
If the Character is under an effect which prevents
the use of Skills, such as Drain, the Character is
unable to attack or defend with these Claws.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Versatile (Choose One; Fast, Magical, Tough)
Versatile (Choose One; Martial, Stealthy, Survivor)
Wild
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Hunter
(Expertise must be higher Priority than Crafting)
Limited Dexterity
(Crafting and Expertise Cannot be above Priority C)
Secutor [Zoan]
The Secutor are humanoids with traits not only
limited to insects, but arachnids, crustaceans and just
about anything found in nature with an exoskeleton.
They are incredibly socially cooperative with little in
terms of private property and privacy. As a result they
all work together doing what their best at to make
things better for everyone.
Secutor adventurers are often in support roles
ranging from healers to front line guardians keeping
not only themselves, but those around them alive.
There do exist the occasional independent sort but
these are relatively rare.
Costuming Requirements: Arthropod traits.
Costuming Suggestions: Secutor almost all wear
armour. This armour tends to be plate and modelled
after the exoskeleton of the invertebrate they
resemble.
Heritage Abilities:
Living Armour: Any Physical Armour worn by the
Character also counts as Natural Armour. This
prevents their armour from being Broken or
Destroyed and, finally, allows the portion of their
Natural Armour which comes from their worn
Physical Armour to be Refit by spells and effects
which target either Physical or Natural Armour.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Versatile (Choose One; Skilled, Tough, Trader)
Versatile (Choose One; Martial, Stealthy, Psychic)
Wild
Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Claws Out
(Expertise or Fighting must be Priority A)
Pacifist
(Fighting cannot be above Priority C)
Verdant [Zoan]
The Verdant are a diverse Heritage of humanoid
plants, and organisms similar to plants. Though these
groups often have distinct names for themselves they
share the same general characteristics. Verdant are
extremely durable and resistant to most harmful
effects making them outstanding protectors of both
nature and the civilised world. They are also quite
ponderous thinking carefully about what they'll say
before they say it. This can sometimes lead to others
believing they are being ignored when the verdant is
simply being cautious about what they'll say.
Verdant adventure to see new places. There may
be people there but it's really the terrain, flora and
features of the natural world that they want to
experience. When they do form a bond with others,
it's extremely strong and long lasting. It's not unusual
for a Verdant to maintain friendship with a family
through several generations.
Costuming Requirements: Leaves or flowers in
hair. Overt plant or fungal traits.
Costuming Suggestions: Verdant dress like most
people decorate their gardens. They have bright
colours, wooden armour pieces and a habit of looking
like a scarecrow which has been reclaimed by nature.
Heritage Abilities:
Verdant Recovery: Once per Reset, while
standing on natural terrain, the Character may
deliver, by Touch to themselves, a Physical
Rejuvenate or Physical Regenerate. If this effect is
interrupted the Character may Meditate to recover the
use. This Ability cannot be used if the Character is
standing on artificial terrain such as indoors, on a
road, or on masoned stone.
The Character may Rest in order to regain the use
of this ability.
Heritage Traits:
(Choose Two or Gain Three and a Drawback)
Tough
Versatile (Choose One; Spiritual, Strong, Tough)
Versatile (Choose One; Magical, Tough, Survivor)

Heritage Drawbacks:
(Choose one only if all Three Heritage Traits are taken)
Limited Dexterity
(Crafting and Expertise Cannot be above Priority C)
Slow and Steady
(Expertise and Fighting Cannot be above Priority C)
Heritage Skill Descriptions
Ambush [Heritage, Stealthy]*
Requirements: None
Benefit: Once per Reset, for every time this Skill is
bought, the Character may grant themselves the
following effects; "Physical Vorpal 3, Physical
Ambush 3".
Break Compulsion [Heritage, Psychic]
Requirements: None
Benefit: A Character with this Skill may spend
sixty(60) seconds, role playing with another creature.
This can either be in quiet concentration or by
speaking with the target. At the end of the minute, the
Character states "Break Compulsion. Arcane Dispel
Compulsion.". This ability is usable at will.
Alternatively, the Character may expend this Skill,
becoming unable to use its benefits until Reset, to
instantly negate all Compulsions on any target,
including themselves, by Touch. When used in this
manner the call is "Arcane Dispel Compulsion". When
used in this way the effect may be delivered as Touch
or by Packet.
Die Hard [Heritage, Survivor]
Requirements: None
Benefit: Once per Reset, while the Character is in
their Death Count they may, at any time, grant
themselves a "Physical Life".
Disrupt Magic [Heritage, Wild]*
Requirements: None
Benefit: Once per Reset, for every time this Skill is
bought, the Character may deliver a "Magic Remove
Magic" effect by Packet or Touch.
Escape Binding [Heritage, Strong]*
Requirements: None
Benefit: Once per Reset, for every time this Skill is
bought, the Character may deliver a "Physical Dispel
Binding" effect on themselves on a three(3)-count.
The call for this ability is "I rip free 1, I rip free 2, I rip
free 3. Physical Remove Binding."
Field Repair [Heritage, Skilled]
Requirements: None
Benefit: The Character may, on a sixty(60)-count,
Repair any number of mundane items without the use
of a Portable Forge or expending any levels of
Smithing. Once this ability is used, the Character
cannot use it again until they return to Town to
acquire replacement materials and maintain their
tools, a process requiring access to a Forge and
five(5) minutes.
If the Character has the Tinkerer Sub-Skill, they may
use this ability an additional time before needing to
Rest.
Fieldwork [Heritage, Trader]
Requirements: None
Benefit: The Character does not require a Crafting
Table to use the special abilities from Create Potion,
Create Scroll, and Smithing.The Character still
requires a Crafting Table in order to use the abilities
granted from other Skills such as Augment Potion, or
Create Wand.
Focus [Heritage, Willful]*
Requirements: None
Benefit: Once per Reset, for every time this Skill is
bought, the Character may ignore Disruption for the
casting of a single Spell. Once the Spell is cast, the
Character must state "Focus" if they were subject to
an effect which normally would have interrupted the
casting.
Frenzy [Heritage, Wild]*
Requirements: None
Benefit: Once per Reset, for every time this Skill is
bought, the Character may either Resist a single
Berserk effect by which they are struck, the call for
which is "Resist. Physical Vorpal 5", or choose to
activate this Skill at any time. When this Skill is
activated at the Character's discretion the call is
"Physical Vorpal 5".
Harvester [Heritage, Trader]
Requirements: None
Benefit: At the start of each Event, the Character
receives one of the following items for each Game
Day of the Event; one(1) Adamantine Ingot, one(1)
Iron Ingot, one(1) Silver Ingot, or a single strength
one(1) Ritual Component.
Last Words [Heritage, Spiritual]
Requirements: None
Benefit: Whenever the Character enters their Death
Count, they may say up to ten(10) words at a normal
speaking volume.
If the Character has Rank 4 of the Seer Skill, they
may use this ability while Manifested.
Lifebind [Heritage, Spiritual]
Requirements: None
Benefit: Once per Reset, the Character may deliver
a "Magic Tarry" effect by Touch. Alternatively, they
may activate this ability on themselves at any time
during their Death Count.
If the Character has Rank 4 of the Seer Skill, this
Skill can be expended to increase the time they may
be Manifested by five(5) minutes.
If the Character has Rank 10 of the Seer Skill, they
may once per Reset, touch a target and deliver a
"Lesser Arcane Imbue Skill: Last Words" allowing the
touched target to use this Skill for ten(10) seconds.
Mercantile Connections [Heritage, Trader]
Requirements: None
Benefit: The Character gains five(5) Merchant draws
each Event.
Mind Over Body [Heritage, Willful]
Requirements: None
Benefit: Once per Reset, the Character remains
Conscious while Dying. This Skill does not grant the
Character the ability to use any Skills while in this
state, but allows the Character to move at a normal
walking rate ignoring all further damage. This effect
ends when the Character is healed to one(1) or more
Hit Points or when they enter their Death Count.
This effect does not grant Immunity to any effects
other than Weapon Damage.
Natural Cunning [Heritage, Stealthy]
Requirements: None
Benefit: The Character gains Cunning 2, increasing
their Weapon Damage, when standing behind their
target and able to see both shoulder blades, by
two(2). This Skill does not count as a prerequisite for
any other Skill. The Cunning from this Skill stacks
with that granted by all effects including Spells.
