Skill in order to deliver a Packet attack, delivered by
any number of packets, which cause a "Lesser
Physical Fetter" to any Character struck by one or
more packets.
This Skill requires the Character to have any
combination of three(3) the following Skills; Acid
Grenade, Fire Grenade, Freezing Grenade, or Toxin
Grenade, for every time this Skill is bought.
Shrapnel Trap [Expertise]*
Requirements: Shrapnel Grenade 2 per Rank
Benefit: Once per Reset, for every time this Skill is
bought, they may lay down a Shrapnel Trap,
represented by a small white coloured cloth, or
similar marker, with an area of no more than 1m
2
which can be of any shape but may not contain voids.
Once the rep has been placed the Character must
spend ten(10) seconds to arm the Trap during which
time they must keep at least one hand on the Trap.
Once activated anyone who steps or touches the
Trap takes "50 Physical Normal" damage.
A Trap can be disarmed by striking it with a Break
or Destroy effect or by its creator spending ten(10)
seconds touching it in order to disarm it. Disarming a
Trap does not trigger its effects. A disarmed Trap is
considered Destroyed.
A Character may have no more than one(1) Trap,
of any type, placed at a time.
Simple Weapon [Weapon Proficiency]
Requirements: None
Benefit: The Character gains the ability to wield all
Simple Weapons in combat. A Simple Weapon can
only be used to block and can never be used to
attack but can be used to deliver a Killing Blow. For
information of the requirements for the Physical
Representations for this, and other Weapons, see
Chapter 5: Equipment.
Slime Armour [Crafting]*
Requirements: None
Benefit: The Character gains five(5) points of Natural
Armour for each time this Skill is bought. A Character
is still limited by their Wear Total which limits the
maximum number of Armour Points of any type from
which the Character may benefit.
Slime Armour is represented by the Character
wearing a number of vials, of any shape, equal to the
total Armour Point value of the armour divided by
ten(10), rounded up. These vials must be attached to
a single accessory which is worn by, and visible
upon, on the Character.
If the Character is under an effect which prevents
them from using Skills, such as the Drain effect, they
are unable to Refit this armour using the Refit Armour
counted action until that effect ends.
Slime Shuck [Crafting]*
Requirements: Level 5 Alchemy Slot
Benefit: Once per Reset, for every time this Skill is
bought, as long as the Character has at least one(1)
Armour Point of Natural Armour available they may
expend all remaining Natural Armour Points to grant
themselves a "Poison Dispel Binding" effect. The call
for this ability is "Absorb. Poison Dispel Binding".
Slimy Coating [Crafting, Mutation]*
Requirements: Level 1 Alchemy Slot
Benefit: Once per Reset, for every time this Skill is
bought, the Character may deliver a single Alchemy
as a <Effect>-Strike using a Weapon, instead of a
Packet, with which they are proficient. The Character
does not need a free hand to cast the Alchemy if they
are wielding a Weapon.
Smithing [Crafting]*
Requirements: None
Benefit: A Character with this Skill may create any
mundane Weapon or suit of Physical Armour. In order
to create these items the Character must have
access to a Forge.
Smithing, Grand Master [Crafting]
Requirements: Master Smithing, Smithing 20
Benefit: This Skill grants the Character the ability to
cast Ritual Smithing. Upon gaining this Skill the
Character can act as the Primary Caster, or as a
Secondary Caster, for any Ritual Smithing.
Additionally, the Character gains the ability to cast
Invocations. See Chapter 8: Rituals for more
information on casting Ritual Smithing and
Invocations.
The Character can also use this Skill to Identify
any number of Ritual Components on a sixty(60)-
count.
Smithing, Master [Crafting]
Requirements: Smithing 10
Benefit: A Character with this Skill may create
Mastercraft Weapons and Physical Armour. These
exceptional Weapons can also include rare and
Special Materials such as Adamantine, Iron and
Silver.
Spell Channelling [Magic]
Requirements: Level 3 Spell Slot, Channel Spell 5
Benefit: This Skill grants the Character the ability to
deliver all 1st, 2nd, and 3rd level Spells, the Character
casts from memory, as an <Effect>-Strike with their
Weapon instead of a Packet. The Delivery Type used
to deliver the effect is determined by the Delivery
Type of the Spell. For example, if the Spell allows the
Character to deliver a Lesser Magic Pin effect, the
call for this ability is "Lesser Magic-Strike Pin". If the
effect causes "20 Piercing Physical Ice" damage, the
Channelled effect is "20 Piercing Physical-Strike Ice".
Additionally, the Character does not need a free hand
to cast Spells if they are wielding a Weapon with
which they are Proficient. They may even Touch Cast
Spells with their Weapon by placing the Weapon on
the target during the Incantation and Delivery Verbal.
For more information on Channelling Spells see
Page 100.
Spell Channelling, Greater [Magic]
Requirements: Level 6 Spell Slot, Spell Channelling
Benefit: This Skill grants the Character the ability to
deliver all 4th, 5th, and 6th level Spells, the Character
casts from memory, as an <Effect>-Strike with their
Weapon instead of a Packet. They do not need a free
hand to cast Spells if they are wielding a Weapon
with which they are Proficient. The Character may
Touch Cast Spells with their Weapon by placing the
Weapon on the target during the Incantation and
Delivery Verbal.
Spell Channelling, Superior [Magic]
Requirements: Level 9 Spell Slot, Greater Spell
Channelling
Benefit: This Skill grants the Character the ability to
deliver all 7th, 8th, and 9th level Spells, the Character
casts from memory, as an <Effect>-Strike with their
Weapon instead of a Packet. They do not need a free
hand to cast Spells if they are wielding a Weapon
with which they are Proficient. The Character may
Touch Cast Spells with their Weapon by placing the
Weapon on the target during the Incantation and
Delivery Verbal.
Additionally, the Character may expend a use of
the Channel Spell Skill in order to allow themselves to
deliver a single copy of any Spell they have delivered
as <Effect>-Strike using this ability if the initial swing
failed to strike any target. The call for this copy is
"Echo: <Delivery Verbal>".
Spell Slot [Magic]*
Requirements: Magical Aptitude, See Text
Benefit: This Character gains one Spell Slot of the
appropriate level as detailed under Pyramid Skills on
Page 32.
Split Shot [Expertise]*
Requirements: One Rank of Stealth Training or
Weapon Training per Rank.
Benefit: Once per Reset, for every time this Skill is
bought, the Character may throw up to five(5) Archery
Packets when using a Bow, or Crossbow, to deliver
an attack. Alternatively, they may throw up to three(3)
Thrown Weapons to deliver the attack. This ability is
usable on both Weapon Damage and can even be
used with Martial Arts and other Skills.
Spy [Background]
Prerequisites: None
Benefit: A Character with this Skill is in the know
about many secrets. At the start of each Event, the
Player receives a rumour sheet.
If the Character has the Open Lock Skill, they
reduce the time needed to disarm a Trap to ten(10)
seconds. If the Trap has a limited number of tries,
they gain one(1) additional attempt.
Staff [Weapon Proficiency]
Requirements: None
Benefit: The Character gains the ability to wield a
Staff in combat. A Staff can only be used to block and
can never be used to attack or deliver any effect. For
information of the requirements for the Physical
Representations for this, and other Weapons, see
Chapter 5: Equipment.
Stealth Prowess [Expertise]*
Requirements: See Text
Benefit: Once per Reset, for every time this Skill is
bought, the Character may grant themselves the
effects of a Vorpal 5. This allows them to increase the
Weapon Damage call of their next five(5) landed hits
by five(5). The call for this ability is "Physical Vorpal
5". Alternatively, the Character may grant themselves
the effects of an Ambush 5, allowing them to use any
Skill, which requires them to be behind their target,
regardless of their position. The call for this ability is
"Physical Ambush 5".
A Character may have a number of Stealth
Prowess that does not exceed five(5) plus one(1) for
each Stealth Training they possess.
Stealth Training [Expertise]*
Requirements: See Text
Benefit: The Character increases the Weapon
Damage call of all Weapons they wield when behind
their target, by two(2) for each Rank they have in this
Skill. A Character is considered to be behind their
target if they are physically standing behind them and
able to see both of their shoulder blades.
There are two ways to purchase this skill. The first
is to trade in a number of ranks of Stealth Prowess
equal to their number of ranks in Stealth Training plus
five(5). The Character does not receive any build
back for the traded in Stealth Prowess ranks as this is
built into the cost for the Skill. The second is to
purchase the Skill outright at a cost which is
increased each time the Character purchases
another Rank. For example, the cost for 3 Ranks of
Stealth Training for a Character with Priority A in
Expertise is 54 (16 + 18 + 20).
Style Master [Weapon Proficiency]
Requirements: None
Benefit: The Character gains the ability to wield all
Shields and the ability to use a single shield in
combat. Additionally, they gain the ability to wield any
One-Handed Weapon, with which they are proficient,
in their off-hand at the same time they wield a
Weapon in their main hand. This Weapon's damage
is increased by the main hand Character's Weapon
Training by an amount equal to the Character's main
hand Weapon Training minus four(4). While this
means the minimum damage bonus is zero(0), the
Character will still cause the Base Damage of the
Weapon they are wielding in their off-hand. At the
time this Skill is bought, the Character may "sell back"
any Skills which are included in its benefit. For
information of the requirements for the Physical
Representations for this, and other Weapons, see
Chapter 5: Equipment.
Sword Mastery [Weapon Proficiency]
Requirements: None
Benefit: The Character gains the ability to wield all
Swords in combat. When wielding a Two-Handed
Weapon, the Character may block attacks while
holding the Weapon with one hand but cannot attack
or use the Weapon to deliver any Skill until they have
both hands on the Two-Handed Weapon. At the time
this Skill is bought, the Character may "sell back" any
Skills which are included in its benefit. For information
of the requirements for the Physical Representations
for this, and other Weapons, see Chapter 5:
Equipment.
Symbiont [Crafting]
Prerequisites: Slime Armour 5
Benefit: A Character with this Skill is able to Cast
Alchemy while under the effects of Binding, except
Confine. Additionally, they no longer need a free hand
to cast Alchemy.
Third Martial Arts School [Fighting]
Requirements: Second Martial Arts School
Benefit: This Skill grants the Character access to
their third Martial Arts School. The Character is able
to expend their Martial Arts Slots to use the Martial
Arts which belong to this school, and any other
School the Character knows. See Chapter 7: Magic
for more information on Martial Arts.
Third School of Magic [Magic]
Requirements: Second School of Magic
Benefit: This Skill grants the Character access to
their third School of Magic. The Character is able to
expend their Spell Slots to use the Spells which
belong to any schools they know. See Chapter 7:
Magic for more information on the Schools of Magic.
Thrown [Weapon Proficiency]
Requirements: None
Benefit: The Character gains the ability to wield a
Thrown Weapons in combat. Thrown Weapons may
never possess a core and must be sculpted only
using foam. Additionally, never aim for illegal areas
including the head, groin and face. For information of
the requirements for the Physical Representations for
this, and other Weapons, see Chapter 5: Equipment.
Toughness [Fighting]*
Requirements: 5 Character Levels per Rank
Benefit: The Character gains five(5) additional Hit
Points for each time this Skill is bought,
Toxication [Crafting]*

they are struck, which would remove one or more
Poison effects such as Remove Poison or Dispel
Poison.

2
which can be of

A Trap can be disarmed by striking it with a Break

Two Weapon Fighting [Weapon Proficiency]
Requirements: Florentine
Benefit: The Character gains the ability to wield any
One-Handed Weapon, with which they are proficient,
in their off-hand at the same time they wield a
Weapon in their main hand. This Weapon's damage
is increased by the main hand Character's Weapon
Training by an amount equal to the Character's main
hand Weapon Training minus four(4). While this
means the minimum damage bonus is zero(0), the
Character will still cause the Base Damage of the
Weapon they are wielding in their off-hand. A
Character with this Skill would need to have at least
five(5) Ranks in Weapon Training before they will
receive any bonus damage to their off-hand Weapon.
Two Weapon Fighting, Greater [Weapon Proficiency]
Requirements: Two Weapon Fighting
Benefit: The Character's damage with their off-hand
Weapon is increased to an amount equal to the
Character's main hand Weapon Training minus
two(2). While this means the minimum damage
bonus is zero(0), the Character may still cause the
Base Damage of the Weapon. A Character with this
Skill would need to have at least three(3) Ranks in
Weapon Training before they will receive any bonus
damage to their off-hand Weapon.
Two Weapon Fighting, Superior [Weapon Proficiency]
Requirements: Greater Two Weapon Fighting
Benefit: The Character's damage with their off-hand
Weapon is increased to an amount equal to the
Character's main hand Weapon Training.
Vanish [Heritage, Stealthy]
Requirements: None
Benefit: Once per Reset, the Character may grant
themselves a "Lesser Physical Aegis" effect. The
Character must be in shadows or darkness to use
this ability.

Benefit: A Character with this Skill is Immune to the
Fumble effect.

Archery Weapon of their choice.

Weapon Damage call of their next five(5) landed hits
by five(5). The call for this ability is
"Physical Vorpal 5"
A Character may have a number of Weapon
Prowess that does not exceed five(5) plus one(1) for
each Weapon Training they possess.
Weapon Mastery [Weapon Proficiency]
Requirements: None
Benefit: The Character gains the ability to wield all
Weapons in combat. When wielding a Two-Handed
Weapon, the Character may block attacks while
holding the Weapon with one hand but cannot attack
or or use the weapon to deliver any Skill until they
have both hands on the Two-Handed Weapon. At the
time this Skill is bought, the Character may "sell back"

Weapon Training [Fighting]*
Requirements: See Text
Benefit: Upon gaining this Skill choose a hand; left or
right. The Character increases the Weapon Damage
call of all Weapons they wield in the chosen hand by
one(1) for every Rank they have in this Skill.
There are two ways to purchase this skill. The first
is to trade in a number of ranks of Weapon Prowess
equal to their number of ranks in Weapon Training
plus three(3). The Character does not receive any
build back for the traded in Weapon Prowess ranks
as this is built into the cost for the Skill. The second is
to purchase the Skill outright at a cost which is
increased each time the Character purchases
another Rank. For example, the cost for 3 Ranks of
Weapon Training for a Character with Priority A in
Fighting is 42 (12 + 14 + 16).
Wear Extra Armour [Fighting]*
Requirements: None
Benefit: The Character increases the maximum
number of Wear Points of Armour the Character can
benefit from by five(5) for each time this Skill is
bought. A Character can benefit from, at most, a total
of fifty(50) points of Armour.
Chapter 5: Equipment
Equipment
Adventurers make use of a variety of different
equipment. This equipment may represent weapons,
armour, or special materials used in the creation of
magical effects. The most common types of
equipment are described below.
Costuming
The game world is not modern, it is a medieval
fantasy setting. Thus modern features such as
zippers, velcro should be avoided. Modern items
such as printed t-shirts, wheelies, digital watches and
cell phones should also be avoided whenever
possible.
Any devices which are needed for medical reasons
such as eye glasses, walking casts, insulin pumps
and wheelchairs are considered to be justified
anachronisms and not subject to these requirements.
Prohibited Items
The use of modern technologies for the purposes
of gaining an advantage in the game are not
permitted. Such devices include, but are not limited
to; night vision goggles, calculators, smartphones,
and similar tools. Additionally, real weapons of any
kind are not permitted to be worn or carried by
Players in attendance at the Event. This includes
pyrotechnics and explosives of all kinds..
Modern armour body armour can not be used as a
Physical Representation for Physical Armour in the
game.
Equipment List
Adamantine Item – Weapons made of this material
use the Weapon Type: Normal. Adamantine items are
Indestructible and cannot be Broken or Destroyed.
Additionally, items made from this material are also
Mastercraft Items and can be enhanced in special
ways using Ritual Smithing effects.
Arrows and Bolts – These items are represented by
packets contained in a Quiver. These packets are
considered items and a Character cannot hold more
than one at a time. This also means that a Character
cannot hold even a single arrow with the same hand
being used to hold their bow.
Augmentation Tome – See Augmentation Tomes on
Page 182.
Axe – An Axe is a bladed weapon which is composed
of a grip, shaft, blade, and, occasionally, a
crossguard. The striking surface of an axe is the head
of the axe. An axe may have more than one head but
all heads must meet the following requirements; the
head of the axe must be covered by an additional
twenty-five(25) millimetres of closed cell foam, the
length of the axe head must follow the size
restrictions on the Table 5.3, and the axe must be
rigid enough that it does not fold over when striking,
or if it does, it must return to its original shape
immediately once pressure is removed. All areas of
the weapon other than the head and grip must be
covered by at least ten(10) millimetres of closed cell
foam.
Bow – A bow is a weapon which allows the Character
to deliver a packet based weapon attack. Bows can
never be used to strike another Player but can be
used to block attacks normally. Bows must be curved
into the shape of a bent bow. Bows may include a
string for carrying but bows of any draw weight may
not be used. Even bows designed for LARPs may not
be used. All bows also include a quiver which holds
its ammunition and is necessary to use archery. The
Bow and Quiver are considered the same weapon for
the purposes of Ritual Effects and Special Materials
such as Iron.
Cauldron – See the Skill Create Cauldron on Page
44.
Coin – This refers to all forms of coin currency. The
valid coins which exist are copper, silver, gold and
platinum. One(1) platinum is worth ten(10) gold.
One(1) gold is worth ten(10) silver. One(1) silver is
worth ten(10) copper. Coins cannot be destroyed by
any effect.
Component – This refers to a special item used in
the creation of Ritual Effects. Components can be
Broken and Destroyed. Components cannot be
Repaired. There are seven types of Components:
Power, Creation, Destruction, Elemental, Spirit, Time,
and Void. A Character with the ability to perform
Rituals can Identify components. The number of
Components needed to create a Ritual Effect is given
in the Ritual Cost portion of its description, though
some may have additional costs. The Component
type Void can be used in place of any Component
type. There are four rarities of Components;
Common, Uncommon, Rare, and Special. These
components have a strength of one(1), two(2), four(4)
for the former three while Special Components are
used for specific Ritual Effects such as the creation of
Legendary Items or Travel to an exact location on
another plane.
Crossbow – A crossbow is a weapon which allows
the Character to deliver a packet based weapon
attack. Crossbows can never be used to strike
another Player but can be used to block attacks
normally. Crossbows must be made into the shape of
a crossbow with a stock with two or more arms
extending perpendicularly from the stock. Crossbows
may include a string for carrying but crossbows of
any draw weight may not be used. Even crossbows
designed for LARPs may not be used. All crossbows
also include a quiver which holds its ammunition. The
Crossbow and Quiver are considered the same
weapon for the purposes of Ritual Effects and Special
Materials such as Iron.
Flask – See the Skill Create Flask on Page 44
In Game Item – This refers to all Weapons, Armour,
Tagged Items, Trinkets, Potions and Scrolls.
<Special Material> Ingot – This item represents a
purified material fit to be used in the creation of
special items. The three common Special Materials
are; Adamantine, Iron and Silver.
Iron Item – Weapons made of this material use the
Weapon Type: Iron in place of Normal. Iron weapons
are most effective against enemies composed of
magical energy such as Fae and Elementals.
Additionally, items made from this material are also
Mastercraft Items and can be enhanced in special
ways using Ritual Smithing effects.
Legendary Item – These Mastercrafted items have
eight(8) slots for Ritual Effects. They can be created
with some Skills but require an investment of one-
hundred(100) gold to produce. They are always
Indestructible.
Mace – A Mace is a blunt weapon which is composed
of a grip, shaft, head, and, occasionally, a
crossguard. The striking surface of a mace is the
head of the mace. A mace may have more than one
head but all heads must meet the following
requirements; the head of the mace must be covered
by an additional twenty-five(25) millimetres of closed
cell foam, the length of the mace head must follow
the size restrictions on the Table 5.3, and the mace
must be rigid enough that it does not fold over when
striking, or if it does, it must return to its original
shape immediately once pressure is removed. All
areas of the weapon other than the head and grip
must be covered by at least ten(10) millimetres of
closed cell foam.
Mastercrafted Item – These items are exceptionally
well made. They have five(5) Slots available for Ritual
Effects. All items made of Special Materials, such as
Adamantine, Iron, and Silver, are always
Mastercrafted Items.
Mundane Item – This refers to a common item made
of common materials. They cannot be made into
Ritual items. Such items do not require an item tag
and a Character begins each event with all items for
which they have a valid Physical Representation.
Mythical Item – These Mastercrafted items have
ten(10) slots for Ritual Effects. They are always
Indestructible.
Physical Armour – This refers to all manufactured
armour from leather, to mail, to splint, to brigandine,
to plate. Physical Armour is manufactured by
Characters with the Smithing Skill, can be
Mastercrafted and even made of special materials
regardless of the type of armour..
Portable Forge – This refers to a prop which
appears to be a small portable blacksmith's forge. It is
up to the Marshal to determine if a representation
meets the requirements and once approved, is
usable for the Event. The forge must include the
following; an anvil, a forge or crucible, and a hammer.
These are the minimum requirements though a
Player is encouraged to expand on these
requirements and be creative in their design.
Potion – This refers to a specially prepared vial or
bottle which contains an imbibable spell effect with a
Range of Touch or Packet. A potion is represented by
a container with a volume between twenty(20) and
one-hundred(100) millilitres. All Potions must contain
a label with the contained Spell's name. In order to
use a Potion the Character must, on a three(3)-count
role-play drinking the Potion. A potion can be given to
another Character who is either willing or helpless. In
this case the Character administering the potion must
spend a three(3)-count role-playing feeding the target
the potion. Potions follow all rules for Touch casting.
They can be Broken or Destroyed. A Potion cannot
be Repaired. All Potions expire at the end of the
Event. For more information see the Skill Create
Potion on Page 45
Quiver – A quiver is a special container which holds
arrows or bolts for use with a bow or crossbow
respectively. A quiver is considered to have unlimited
ammunition but a Character must have one in order
to use archery attacks. A quiver and bow are
considered to be the same weapon for the purposes
of Ritual Effects and items made from special
materials.
Ritual Item – This refers to any Item which is imbued
with one(1) or more Ritual Effects. All such Items are
automatically Indestructible and Immune to Break
and Destroy Effects. A Character may benefit from
the abilities of no more than five(5) Ritual Items at a
time. In order to use a Ritual Item a Character must
Attune to it. In order to Attune to a Ritual Item the
Character must know all effects of the Item to which
they wish to attune, spend a sixty(60) second Count
to Attune to the item. If a Character is already Attuned
to five(5) Ritual Items they must first spend a
sixty(60) second Count to Unattune to one(1) Ritual
Item first. During a Rest, the Character may
Unattune, or Attune to any number of Ritual items.
Rune – See the Skill Rune Carving on Page 55.
Scroll – This refers to a specially prepared piece of
paper, parchment or vellum which contains a single
Spell with a Range of Touch or Packet. In order to
cast the spell contained in the Scroll a Character
must be able to read the Scroll, touch a packet to it,
and cast the spell with its full Incantation. A Character
cannot use their own Spellshape Skills on a spell cast
from a Scroll. All scrolls must include the name of the
spell it contains but it is recommended that the
Character who created it also include the School and
Level if they are going to share them with other
Characters. All scrolls must have an area of at least
40cm
2
. They can be Broken or Destroyed. A Scroll
cannot be Repaired. All Scrolls expire at the end of
the Event. For more information see the Skill Create
Scroll on Page 45
Shield – Shields are special, defensive only weapons
which can be used to block Weapon Attacks. While
they are under the category of "weapon" they do not
count as Weapons for any effect which targets
weapons such as the Enhance Weapon Ritual Effect
or even the Fumble <Weapon> effect. A Character
can wield no more than one shield at a time. See
Page 69 for specific rules on Shields.
Silver Item – Weapons made of this material use the
Weapon Type: Silver in place of Normal. Silver
weapons are most effective against enemies which
are abominations of nature such as Undead, Oozes
and Shapeshifters. Additionally, items made from this
material are also Mastercraft Items and can be
enhanced in special ways using Ritual Smithing
effects.
Sword – A Sword is a weapon which is composed of
a grip, a blade, and, usually, a crossguard. The
striking surface of a sword is the length of the
weapon above the crossguard and this entire length
must be covered with at least fifteen(15) millimetres
of closed cell foam.
Staff – A Staff is a special weapon which does not
require any Proficiency to wield. A Staff can be used
only for blocking and can never be used to deliver
any attack or effect. The entire length of the weapon
is considered a non-striking surface.
Steel Item – Weapons made of this material use the
Weapon Type: Normal. Normal weapons are effective
against most enemies. Items made from this material
can be made Mastercraft Items and enhanced in
special ways using Ritual Smithing effects.
Tagged Item – This refers to all items which have an
Item Tag associated with it. This includes all
Mastercraft items as well as all items with one or
more Ritual Effects.
Thrown Weapon- A thrown weapon is one which is
composed entirely out of closed cell foam. Thrown
weapons may not contain a rigid core of any kind.
Finally, a thrown weapon may not be used in combat
except by throwing. This means that they cannot be
used to block attacks, attack in melee or even be
used to deliver a Killing Blow.
Trap – This refers to both a trap created by a
Character's Skills, as well as a hazard put into play
by Plot. A Trap created with a Skill is a coloured piece
of cloth which is attached to a surface. Any character
who touches the cloth suffers the effects of the trap.
Traps can be Broken or Destroyed. For more
information on Traps see Page 67.
When a Trap is created as a Plot effect it may
follow special rules which will be explained by the
Marshal. Generally speaking, a trap will require a
Character to have a Skill which allows them to open
locks to attempt to disarm them but this varies based
on the encounter.
Trinket – This refers to Tagged Items which have a
Coin value allowing them to be sold to the bank.
Wand – See the Skill Create Wand on Page 45.
War Staff – A War Staff is a Two-Handed Mace.
Weapons – These items represent a weapon used in
battle. Under no circumstances is a player permitted
to make use of any real weapons and doing so will
result in their immediate removal from the Event.
Smithing
Craft Mastercraft Item
Prerequisites: Master Smithing
A Smith may expend levels of Smithing in order to
produce an item of exceptional quality, or one made
of Special Materials. A Character using this ability
may expend five(5) levels of Smithing to create an
exceptionally well crafted item from normal steel.
Alternatively, the Smith may expend five(5) levels of
Smithing to create an item using a Special Material.
Special Materials include, but are not necessarily
limited to; Adamantine, Iron and Silver. As an
additional cost, the item requires three(3) Ingots of
the Special Material to be used in the item's
construction. These Ingots are consumed by the item.
An item cannot be made of more than one Special
Material. All items created using this ability are
suitable targets for Ritual effects. A Smith can only
create Weapons, including shields and quivers, and
Physical Armour using this Skill.
Craft Mundane Item
Prerequisites: Smithing
A Smith may craft any number of mundane weapons
or armours so long as they have access to a Town
Forge. This ability is usable at will.
Field Mend
Prerequisites: Smithing
In order to use this ability the Smith must have
access to a Portable Forge. The Smith may expend
one(1) level of Smithing in order to Repair any
number of mundane weapons or armour or a single
Mastercraft Weapon or Armour. This allows the Smith
to restore an item which has been Broken. Items
which have been Destroyed cannot be Repaired by
this, or any other effect. This process takes five(5)
minutes during which time the Player must role-play
the use of the forge and the Repair of the items.
Full Repair
Prerequisites: Smithing
In order to use this ability the Smith must have
access to a Town Forge. The Smith may use this
ability to Repair any number of mundane weapons or
armours without expending any levels of Smithing.
Alternatively they may expend one(1) level of
Smithing to Repair a single Mastercraft Weapon or
Armour. Finally they may also expend one(1) level of
Smithing to restore a Reinforced Item's Resist Break
or Destroys. This process takes five(5) minutes
during which time the Player must role-play the use of
the forge and the Repair of the items.
Rapid Refit
Prerequisites: Smithing
The Smith may, expend one(1) level of Smithing in
order to, on a three(3)-count, restore a suit of
Physical Armour, even one worn by another
Character, to full value. The call for this ability is
"Physical Refit Physical Armour".
Reinforce Item
Prerequisites: Master Smithing
A Smith may expend levels of Smithing in order to
reinforce an item making it more resistant to being
damaged. The Smith may expend one(1) level of
Smithing, and one Adamantine Ingot to grant an item
the ability Resist two(2) Break or Destroy effects each
Reset. Alternatively, they may expend three(3) levels
of Smithing, and two(2) Adamantine Ingots to grant
an item the ability Resist four(4) Break or Destroy
effects each Reset. Finally, they may expend six(6)
levels of Smithing, and three(3) Adamantine Ingots to
grant an item the ability Resist six(6) Break or
Destroy effects each Reset. These effects are on the
item and are used automatically against any
applicable effects regardless of the wielder's status or
desires. If an item with this property is Broken and is
Repaired, it regains one(1) use of this ability once
Repaired.
Explosives
Bombs – These items are represented by a handful
of packets. When used, the Character may throw any
number of packets, using one hand, and all at once.
Any target struck by one or more packets takes the
effect of the bomb. No device or method of delivery,
other than packets, is acceptable to deliver the
effects of a Bomb.
Grenades – These items represent a small explosive
device which harms only the first target with which it
is struck. Traditionally, they are represented by a
single packet, but they may also be represented by a
small thrown weapon measuring no more than
eight(8) centimetres in its longest dimension. Like all
weapons, these special grenades must pass
inspection at the start of each Event.
Traps – The Physical Representations for Traps
follow the following requirements; the minimum
dimension of any Trap is thirty(30) centimetres. Any
recesses in the Trap's representation, such as holes,
count against the total area. The maximum total area
of any standard Trap is one(1) square metre, or ten-
thousands(10,000) centimetres. The maximum total
area of any Trap modified by the Skill Giant Trap is
four(4) square metres, or ten-thousands(40,000)
centimetres. There are only five colours which can be
used for the Physical Representations of Trap; blue,
green, red, white, and yellow. No other colours can
be used. A marking in a contrasting colour to indicate
the type of Trap may also be added.
Once a Trap is set it may not be moved. But the
Character who set the Trap may adjust the Trap to
return it to its original position without triggering, or
needing to disarm, the Trap.
Trap Colour Effect
Blue Physical Web
Green 20 Poison Body
Red 20 Physical Fire
White 50 Physical Normal
Yellow 20 Physical Acid

Table 5.3 – Weapon Sizes
Weapons
In order to ensure the safety of all Players, no real
weapons are permitted to be used at any time. All
Weapon Physical Representations a Player brings to
the game must be inspected and approved for use by
a Weapon Marshal at each event. Any unapproved
weapon representations must be removed from site
or placed in a designated out of game area such as
under the bed or in a vehicle.
Weapon Size – The size of the weapon. One-
Handed weapons can be wielded with one hand. In
order for a Character to wield a weapon they must
have the appropriate Weapon Proficiency. The size of
the weapon also determines the diameter of the core
needed for the weapon.
Base Damage – This represents the base damage
caused by the weapon. There are a variety of ways to
increase this damage such as Skills, Rituals and
special abilities.
Overall Length – The weapon may not be shorter
than the lower value and cannot exceed the higher
value. The tolerance for this value is one(1)
centimetre.
Blade/Head Length – This is the striking surface of
the weapon. All striking surfaces of the weapon must
be covered with closed cell foam measuring a
minimum of fifteen(15) millimetres in thickness from
the core to the surface of the weapon.
Non-Striking Length – This is the part of the weapon
which may not be used for striking but may
unintentionally come into contact with another person
while in play. This applies to every part of the weapon
other than the grip and the striking surface. Staves,
Simple Weapons, Bows, and Crossbows are
composed entirely of non-striking length and as such,
can never be used to strike a person. All non-striking
surfaces must be covered with closed cell foam
measuring a minimum of nine(9) millimetres in
thickness.
Grip Length – This is the part of the weapon where
the wielder of the weapon grips the weapon. It does
not need to be covered by any foam but should be
covered with some form of grip.
Shields
Shields come in countless forms but there
are four categories of shields. All shields must meet
the requirements of one or more categories to be
used in play. All shields have one or more points of
contact. A point of contact is a handle, strap or other
fastener which the wielder uses to keep the shield
under their control while in use.
Shields are a purely defensive weapon which can
never, under any circumstances, be used to attack.
Shields do not count as Weapons for any effect which
targets weapons such as the Enhance Weapon
Ritual Effect or even the Fumble <Weapon> effect.
Anyone who strikes another player with a Shield will
lose access to the Shield skill or will face other
disciplinary action. A Character can never wield more
than one shield at a time unless an ability explicitly
states it gives them the ability to do so.
Shields may have items attached or otherwise
carried on the back of the Shield provided these
items meet the following requirements. Firstly, the
item cannot extend beyond the borders of the Shield
in any way. Secondly, the items must be positioned,
and secured, in such a way that they will not
accidentally come into contact with the wielder or any
opponent or their weapons. If either of these
requirements are not met the Shield cannot be used
to defend against attacks and any attack or effect
which strikes the Shield is treated as if it struck the
Character directly.
The arm used to carry a Shield cannot be used to
hold any other Weapon or item unless it is attached
as described above.
The edges of shields must be covered with closed
cell foam which measures a minimum of fifteen(15)
millimetres in thickness when measured from the
edge. No shield may have sharp edges or protruding
features which are rigid. Additionally, shields which
include a boss or other protruding feature must have
all such protrusions covered with a minimum of
ten(10) millimetres of closed cell foam. Shields face
scrutiny in construction methods in order to minimise
injury in the event of accidental contact with another
player, but also to minimise damage to weapons
which strike it.

Shield Sizes
Shields are limited in size according to their type
as defined in Table 5.4. Maximum length is the
maximum distance which can be drawn from any two
points on the edges of the shield.
Shield Area is measured as the total area bounded
by a line drawn around the edges of the shield as if
string was pulled tightly around the shield's edges. No
hollows or recesses in the shield's design are
considered.
Buckler – A buckler is a small shield which is
strapped to the forearm. The primary benefit of the
buckler is that it allows the Character to keep the
hand free to cast spells or hold, and use, other Items
including weapons. In order to wield more than one
weapon the Character must have the appropriate
Skills to do so. This means that a Character may
wield a Buckler on the same hand that holds a bow,
while wielding a two-handed weapon or even while
fighting with two Weapons. This does not allow a
Character to wield more than one Shield at a time. All
bucklers must have an area according to the below
table.
Gauntlet – A gauntlet is a special shield represented
by an obvious gauntlet on the Character's off-hand
extending from the hand to at least three-quarters up
their forearm. The Character may block weapon
damage which strikes the arm wearing the gauntlet
as if their entire arm were a shield. A character
wielding a Gauntlet cannot use the arm wearing it to
wield any weapon, shield, or even hold any in-game
item without losing the benefits of the gauntlet.
Finally, the hand wearing the gauntlet is not
considered free for the purposes of spell casting.
Small Shield – This type of shield has one or more
points of contact and is the largest type of shield
which can be a hold out shield. It may be held either
parallel or perpendicular to the arm. All small shields
must have an area according to the above table.
Large Shield – This type of shield is strapped to the
arm with at least two points of contact, usually a strap
and a handle. Regardless of the method used to
secure the shield it must be held parallel to the
forearm. All large shields must have an area
according to the below table.
Shield Type Maximum Length Minimum Area Maximum Area Grip Requirement

Table 5.4 – Shield Sizes
Armour
Physical Armour
This armour is any which is manufactured and
created by the Smithing Skill. It is the most common
form of armour being made of toughened hide or
metal. It comes in a variety of forms and grants
Armour Points based on its material and the parts of
the body it covers. In order for armour to be
considered covering an area it must cover a total of
75% of the total area. The Armour Points granted by
coverage of each area can be found in the table
below. As an item, Physical Armour is a valid target
for the Destroy effect. If the armour is Indestructible, it
is instead reduced to zero(0) Armour Points, when
struck by a Destroy effect, and must be Refit.
Physical Armour is Refit by spending a sixty(60)-
second count role-playing adjusting straps, plates
and armour sections in order to ensure proper
coverage. It can also be Refit by the Refit Physical
Armour effect, or the Refit All Armour effect.
Should armour cover less than 75% of the area,
but at least 50% of the area the armour still grants
some protection, but it counts as one armour type
lower than normal. For example, a piece of Plate
armour which covers only the lower torso, such as a
gut plate, the armour counts instead as Mail. Leather
Armour counts as one(1) point less for all sections.
Representations for Physical Armour
All Armour must be free of rigid protrusions which
may accidentally damage weapons, or harm other
players when struck. Additionally, all armour must
appear to be in period. Corrugated plastic sign
boards with no attempt to disguise their nature will
not be considered valid armour.
As with weapons, all armour must be approved
prior to being used in game. This is to ensure that the
suit has the correct Armour Point value assigned and
it is free from potentially dangerous features.
Leather
Leather Armour is represented by tough, but
flexible, materials such as leather, rope, suede, vinyl
and similar flexible materials as well as materials
which convincingly appear to be these materials.
Boiled Leather, and armour which has been
toughened in some way so as to be exceptionally
rigid instead counts as Mail. Exceptionally impressive
leather armour can also be counted as Mail.
Examples are; leather armour, leather scale, rope
armour and gambeson.
Mail

Mail armour refers to a mesh of interlocking pieces
made of rigid materials which remain flexible due to
the way they are interconnected. This includes
chainmail as well as leather armours which
incorporate rigid materials over most of its area.
Examples are; Chainmail, ring armour, some forms of
splint mail.
Plate
Plate Armour refers to armour which is composed
of rigid overlapping plates of armour as well as
materials which convincingly appear to be metal. The
armour must appear to be rigid in order to count as
Plate. Examples include; breastplate, full plate, scale
mail, brigandine and lamellar.
Armour Location Leather Mail Plate
Head (Scalp) 2 4 6
Front Torso 3 6 9
Back Torso 3 6 9
Upper Arm (Each) 2 3 4
Forearm (Each) 1 2 4
Upper Leg (Each) 2 3 4
Lower Leg (Each) 1 2 4
Table 5.5 – Armour Locations
Representations for Arcane, Avoidance and
Slime Armour
In order to provide a subtle, but distinct visual clue
that a person is benefiting from invisible armour, and
to avoid accidental accusations of not taking damage,
the three types of non-physical armour a Player
Character can possess must be represented as
follows.
Arcane Armour

The Character must wear a number of distinct
runes, which are visible from a distance of two(2)
metres, equal to the amount of Arcane Armour the
Character is wearing divided by ten(10) rounded up.
This means a Character with thirty-five(35) points of
Arcane Armour must wear four(4) such runes as part
of their costuming. The runes must be worn as part of
a single accessory. The most common form of
costuming is a necklace with a number of charms
depicting a particular rune.
Avoidance Armour
The Character must wear a number of feathers,
which are visible from a distance of two(2) metres,
equal to the amount of Avoidance Armour the
Character is wearing divided by ten(10) rounded up.
This means a Character with fifteen(15) points of
Avoidance Armour must wear two(2) such feathers as
part of their costuming. The feathers must be worn as
part of a single accessory. The most common form of
costuming is a necklace, or armband, with a number
of feathers.
Slime Armour

The Character must wear a number of vials, which
are visible from a distance of two(2) metres, equal to
the amount of Natural Armour the Character is
wearing divided by ten(10) rounded up. This means a
Character with fifty(50) points of Slime Armour must
wear five(5) such vials as part of their costuming. The
vials must be worn as part of a single accessory. The
most common form of costuming is a necklace with a
number of small flasks hanging from it.
Chapter 6: Combat
Combat
All persons participating in this game consent to
being struck by weapons which have been approved
for use in Crucible. Any person who is unable to be
struck by weapons due to medical issues may still
participate but are limited to a non-combatant role.
While accidental contact may occur, it is not
acceptable to engage in non-consensual physical
contact of any kind. Whenever possible contact
should be done through weapons or packets.
Non-Combatants
Some people are unable to engage in combat due
to medical conditions or being too young. All such
people must wear a yellow headband which is visible
from all directions. No person shall strike any person
with a yellow headband with a weapon for any
reason. All that is required to incapacitate such a
person is to say "I drop you." while standing within
weapon's reach. Any ability which negates a weapon
attack such as a Physical Shield, Parry or Dodge can
be used by the non-combatant to negate a single call
of "I drop you.". Any Character, wielding a weapon or
shield, may defend a non-combatant so long as they
are within weapon's reach of the non-combatant. The
call for defending a non-combatant is simply "I defend
you.".
Once the target has been dropped, they
immediately begin Dying.
Delivery Verbals and Ability Calls
Almost all effects in Crucible have a Delivery
Verbal or "Call" associated with them. Everything
from Spells, to Alchemy, to defensive skills like Resist
Poison have a requirement for the Player to say their
associated verbals. All Delivery Verbals must be
spoken in a clear voice loud enough for their target to
hear the effect. This does not mean screaming as
loud as possible, merely speaking them so others can
hear and recognize the effect the Character is using.
All abilities require their full Delivery Verbal to be said
prior to swinging any weapon, or throwing any
packet. While we all occasionally get excited or make
mistakes any Player who is consistently delivering the
attack before completing the Delivery Verbal may be
subject to disciplinary actions.
Counted Actions in Combat
Many actions require a certain amount of time to
perform. Actions are either silent, such as Refitting
breached Armour or Meditating, to actions which
must be counted out loud. In general, all counts
which are more than ten(10) seconds in length are
silent meaning that the Player does not need to count
out loud. All three(3)-counts, counts lasting three(3)
seconds, must be said out loud in a clear speaking
voice. The use of any Skill, or ability, during a
Counted Action of any kind interrupts the Action.
Abilities which are passive such as Immunity, or
Physical Resistance, never interrupt Counted Actions.
Additionally, some Counted Actions have additional
restrictions which may interrupt the Counted Action.
For example, a Character cannot run while Refitting
Armour and being struck by any harmful effect to
which the Character is not Immune interrupts a Killing
Blow.
Attacking with Weapons
Crucible uses a light touch combat system where
all an attacker has to do is strike the body of their
target with a weapon. In order to reduce the likelihood
of injury Crucible imposes the following restrictions on
weapon use.
● All weapon swings should fall between 45
and 90 degrees. Excessive force when
striking with a weapon is not allowed.
● Weapons which have been explicitly
approved for thrusting may be used for
thrusting attacks. No other weapon may be
used for thrusting.
● All attacks must be called prior to each
weapon attack. This means that damage
must be called out with every swing and the
Delivery Verbal for weapon delivered effects
must be finished before starting your swing.
Attacks must be announced in a clear voice
loud enough to be heard by the target.

● It is not acceptable to strike an opponent in
the head (from the neck up), the hands (from
the wrist out) or the groin. Any strikes to
these areas do not count.

● Any person intentionally, or repeatedly,
violating these rules will be subject to
disciplinary action ranging from suspension
of weapon skills to being ejected from play.
Blocking Weapon Attacks
In Crucible a weapon is considered blocked if the
defending Character prevents the attacker's weapon
from coming into contact with their body, usually by
interposing their own weapon or shield between
themselves and the attacker's body. In order to block
an attack the Character must be Proficient with the
Weapon, or Shield, with which they are blocking. If
the Character is under an effect that prevents them
from using Weapons, such as Drain or Web, they are
unable to block Weapons.
● If any part of the attacker's weapon's striking
surface comes into contact with the
defending Character's body the attack is not
blocked.
● Packet attacks, except Archery Packets,
cannot be blocked.
● <Effect>-Strikes, such as Poison-Strikes and
Physical-Strikes, cannot be blocked.
● Weapon Damage which uses the Weapon
Type: Massive cannot be blocked, unless the
Character has a specific ability to do so.
Fighting with Two Weapons
Characters with the Florentine or Two Weapon
Fighting Skills have the ability to fight with two One-
handed Weapons at the same time. A Character with
Florentine is able to wield, in their off-hand, any Short
Weapon which includes, Short Axe, Short Mace and
Short Sword. A Character with Two Weapon Fighting
is able to wield any One-Handed Weapon in their off-
hand. This ability never allows a Character to wield
two Shields. A Character's Off-hand damage is
determined by their Weapon Training and their Skills
according to the following table.
Unless explicitly stated otherwise by the individual
ability description, a Character can perform Martial
Arts and other Weapon Delivered attacks using either
their main hand or their off-hand.
Fighting with Two-Handed Weapons
Characters fighting with a Two-Handed Weapon
are able to block attacks so long as they have at least
one hand on the weapon. A Character may wield, and
attack with a second weapon if, and only, if they have
the Skill Two-Weapon Fighting. They may block
attacks with the Two-Handed Weapon.
When attacking with a Two-Handed Weapon, the
Character must have both hands on the Weapon at
all times. Additionally, the Character must have both
hands on the weapon in order to use any Skills
requiring the use of the weapon except for Parry and
Counter.
Killing Blows
A Killing Blow is a three(3)-counted action which
requires the attacker to place a weapon, with which
they are Proficient, against the torso of their target.
The target must be either willing, or in a state where
they are helpless such as; Sleeping, Paralyzed,
Unconscious, under the effects of a Confine or Web,
or otherwise be unable to move on their own. Once
the Killing Blow count has begun it can be interrupted
by the weapon losing contact with the target, being
struck by a harmful effect to which they are not
Immune, or any Character able to reach the attacker,
with weapon or within arm's reach, saying "I block
you!". Once the three(3)-count has been finished the
attacker states the Weapon Type of their weapon,
such as; Iron, Silver, or Normal (Steel and
Adamantine). As well as any Damage Type they can
add. If the weapon, or the attacker, has the ability to
deliver its Killing Blows as Magic this must also be
included in the call. If successful, the target dies and
immediately enters their Death Count. The call for
this ability is "Killing Blow One, Killing Blow Two,
Killing Blow Three.".
A Killing Blow can also be delivered with a packet
with the packet acting as the weapon.
Example Killing Blows
"Killing Blow One, Killing Blow Two, Killing Blow
Three. Magic, Silver, Ice"
Character Skill Off-Hand Damage
Florentine Base Weapon Damage (Cannot be increased)
Two Weapon Fighting (Ranks in Weapon Training – 4 (min zero(0)) +
Base Weapon Damage.
Greater Two Weapon Fighting (Ranks in Weapon Training – 2 (min zero(0)) +
Base Weapon Damage.
Superior Two Weapon Fighting Ranks in Weapon Training + Base Weapon Damage.
Table 6.1 – Two Weapon Fighting
Attacking with Packets
Crucible uses packets made of cloth and birdseed
and closed with elastic to represent a variety of
effects ranging from spells to arrow to poisons. Most
ranged abilities make use of packets which are
subject to the following rules. Individual Sites may
have specific rules restricting colours which may be
used such as limiting colours to brown and green.
● Packet strikes on any part of the target from
their body to their immediate possessions,
such as cloaks, count.
● The Delivery Verbal for any packet attack
must be completed before the packet is
thrown.
● Do not use excessive force when throwing
packets.
● Effects which are delivered as Touch require
the user to touch the target with a packet.
● Effects which are delivered using multiple
Packets affect each target struck by one or
more packets. Should the effect be Reflected
by one or more targets, the Character who
delivered the effect may expend a single
applicable Defensive Skill to negate every
Reflected effect regardless of how many
targets Reflected the effect.
Archery Packets
In order to represent arrows and bolts, Crucible
uses Archery Packets. Attacking with these packets
follow special rules. In order to fire a shot from their
Bow the Character must remove an arrow,
represented by an Archery Packet, from their Quiver.
Then touch the Packet to the hand holding the Bow
and then role-play the drawing of the arrow back
before throwing the Packet. For information on the
requirements for the Physical Representations for
Bows and Quivers, see Chapter 5: Equipment.
● Archery Packets can be blocked using
Weapons and Shields but otherwise strikes
on any part of the target from their body to
their immediate possessions, such as cloaks,
count.
● These packets are considered items and a
Character cannot hold more than one at a
time. This also means that a Character
cannot hold even a single arrow with the
same hand being used to hold their bow.
● The Delivery Verbal for any packet attack
must be completed before the packet is
thrown.
● Do not use excessive force when throwing
packets.
Being Behind Targets
In Crucible, certain abilities can only be used when
a Character is behind their opponent. A Character
cannot lean over or around their target and be
considered behind their target. To be considered
"Behind" a target the Player must meet all of the
following requirements:
● The Character must have both feet behind
their target's back.
● The Character must, if all obstructions were
removed, be able to see both shoulder
blades. Being able to see only one because
of their target's body being turned so that
only one shoulder is visible is not sufficient.
Alternatively, some abilities, such as Ambush, allow
a Character to ignore any positional requirements for
a limited amount of time, or for limited purposes as
explicitly stated by the Skill, or ability, description.
As long as the Character meets the above
requirements, they may activate abilities which
require them to be "Behind" their target. If their target
turns, or the Character moves in such a way as they
are no longer "behind" their target, the ability is not
interrupted so long as the Character has already
started the ability's Delivery Verbal. For example, if a
Character starts their damage call, increased with
Stealth Training, and their opponent turns part way
through they may finish the damage call as well as
the attack, but would not be able to keep benefiting
from increased damage until they are, again, behind
their target.
End of Combat
Certain effects, such as Aura Spells, end at the
end of Combat. Combat in Crucible is considered to
have ended when any of the following are true:
● The Character goes five(5) minutes without
attacking, or being attacked.
● All enemies have left the site of the combat
and cannot be pursued, or the Character
chooses not to pursue.
● The Character leaves the area via an effect
such as Teleport, Rift, or Gate, unless
explicitly informed otherwise by the
encounter Marshal.
● The Character Dissipates and leaves to seek
Resurrection.
● The encounter Marshal explicitly states
combat has ended.
Weapon Damage
The most common attack in play is weapon
damage. This attack is delivered by weapon and
packet and follows the following format:

< Weapon Type>
10 Normal Sleep
<Damage> <Carrier>
In the above case the attack causes ten(10) points
of damage. The Weapon Type is Normal and the
attack includes a Carrier Attack of Sleep.
Damage: Weapon damage is first subtracted from
any Fortifications Points the Character may have. Any
leftover damage is subtracted from any Armour
Points the Character may have and finally any
remaining damage is subtracted from the Character's
Hit Points.
Weapon Type: While "Normal" is the common
Weapon Type some creatures require special
materials to harm. The following are a list of the
Weapon Types;
● Normal: Affects most living creatures.

● Silver: Affects certain transformed creatures
that were once living such as undead and
werewolves.

● Iron: Affects certain magical creatures such
as Fae and Elementals.

● Massive: This special Weapon Type cannot
be blocked by weapons or shields. It will
affect any creature unless explicitly stated
otherwise.
Carrier: This is an effect which affects the target if
the target's Hit Points are reduced. Carrier attacks
are considered to be Physical in nature. If the
Character is under an effect which allows them to
ignore Weapon Damage, such as the Skill Mind Over
Body, they are not Immune to the effects of the
Carrier if they would otherwise be affected by the
Carrier.
All Weapon Damage is considered Physical in nature
and can be defended against by any effect which
negates an effect delivered as Physical.
Defending Against Attacks
While personal skill helps to avoid many attacks,
there are a number of abilities which can supplement
a Character's ability to avoid attacks.
Negating Attacks
Any attack which is defended against by a
Defensive Ability which "Negates" the effect is
completely negated. For example a weapon attack
causing "10 Normal Sleep" which is defended by a
Resist Sleep ability is completely negated, not just
the Sleep effect.

Types of Defences
Automatic Defences

These defences are triggered by the first effect, to
which they are applicable, which strikes the target. If
a Character has multiple defences which are
applicable to an effect by which they are struck the
Player chooses which is triggered. A Character with
an automatic defence cannot choose for it not to be
triggered unless the effect is delivered by Touch.
Choice Defences
The defences are triggered at the Character's
discretion and can be used to negate any effect to
which it is applicable. If a Character has multiple
defences which are applicable to an effect, by which
they are struck, the Player chooses which, if any, is
triggered. A Character can also choose not to activate
any Choice Defences after being struck by an
applicable effect.
Defensive Abilities
Absorb <Effect> – This ability negates an effect, to
which it is applicable, and grants the character a
charge in some ability as described by the Effect.
Unless stated otherwise by the source of this ability,
this is considered a "Choice" defence allowing the
Character to choose when to activate this ability. The
call for this ability is "Absorbed".
Block – This ability negates a single attack causing
Weapon Damage. Unless stated otherwise by the
source of this ability, this is considered a "Choice"
defence allowing the Character to choose when to
activate this ability. The call for this ability is "Block".
Counter – In order to use this defence the Character
must be wielding a weapon, or shield, with which they
are proficient, and not be under any effect, such as
Fumble, Weakness, Drain, or Cripple, which would
keep them from wielding that weapon with that hand.
This ability can be used to negate any effect which
causes Weapon Damage, or is delivered as Physical,
or <Delivery Type>-Strike. This ability causes the
attack to not only be negated, but Reflected back
against the attacker. An effect which is Reflected
follows all the same rules as the original attack. Any
effect which is reflected twice is fully negated. Unless
stated otherwise by the source of this ability, this is
considered a "Choice" defence allowing the
Character to choose when to activate this ability. The
call for this ability is "Reflect".
Dodge – This ability can be used to negate any effect
which is delivered by Weapon or Packet. Unless
stated otherwise by the source of this ability, this is
considered a "Choice" defence allowing the
Character to choose when to activate this ability.
Effects delivered as Arcane(-Strike) cannot be
avoided by this, or any other, ability. The call for this
ability is "Dodge".
Evasion – This ability can be used to negate any
effect which would affect the target. Unless stated
otherwise by the source of this ability, this is
considered a "Choice" defence allowing the
Character to choose when to activate this ability.
Effects delivered as Arcane(-Strike) cannot be
avoided by this, or any other, ability. The call for this
ability is "Evasion".
Immunity to <Effect> – This ability completely
negates the effect to which it is applicable. The call
for this ability is "No Effect". Effects which are
delivered as Arcane(-Strike) bypass any Immunity the
Character may possess.
Parry – In order to use this defence the Character
must be wielding a weapon, or shield, with which they
are proficient, and not be under any effect, such as
Fumble, Weakness, Drain, or Cripple, which would
keep them from wielding that weapon with that hand.
This ability can be used to negate any effect which
causes Weapon Damage, or is delivered, by weapon
or packet, as Physical, or <Delivery Type>-Strike.
Unless stated otherwise by the source of this ability,
this is considered a "Choice" defence allowing the
Character to choose when to activate this ability.
Effects delivered as Arcane(-Strike) cannot be
avoided by this, or any other, ability. The call for this
ability is "Parry".
Resist <Effect> – This ability negates an effect, to
which it is applicable. This ability cannot be used
against any effect which is delivered as Piercing.
Unless stated otherwise by the source of this ability,
this is considered a "Choice" defence allowing the
Character to choose when to activate this ability. The
call for this ability is "Resist".
Reflect <Effect> – This ability negates an effect, to
which it is applicable, and causes it to rebound
against the attacker. An effect which is Reflected
follows all the same rules as the original attack. Any
effect which is reflected twice is fully negated. Unless
stated otherwise by the source of this ability, this is
considered a "Automatic" defence being activated
automatically against the first effect, to which it is
applicable, by which the Character is struck. The call
for this ability is "Reflect".
<Effect> Resistance – This passive defence has two
effects. Any effect, to which it is applicable, which
causes damage has its damage reduced by half,
rounded down. Any effect, to which it is applicable,
with a duration other than instant, has its duration
reduced to ten(10) seconds. This ability cannot be
used against any effect which is delivered as
Piercing. The call for this ability is "Reduced".
<Effect> Shield – This ability negates the first effect,
to which it is applicable. This ability cannot be used
against any effect which is delivered as Piercing. This
is considered an "Automatic" defence being activated
automatically against the first effect, to which it is
applicable, by which the Character is struck. The call
for this ability is "<Effect> Shield".
<Effect> Ward – This ability negates a single effect,
to which it is applicable. Unless stated otherwise by
the source of this ability, this is considered a "Choice"
defence allowing the Character to choose when to
activate this ability. The call for this ability is "Ward".
Armour Points
Armour points represent a layer of protection
around a Character's body. Most damage is
subtracted from Armour Points before it causes any
reduction in Hit Points. Armour which has been
targeted by a Break <Armour>, Breach <Type>
Armour or Destroy effect is instantly reduced to
zero(0) Armour Points. Once armour is reduced to
zero(0) Armour Points any remaining damage is
subtracted from the Character's Hit Points.
Each Player Character is created with the ability to
benefit from a maximum of twenty(20) Armour Points.
This is the Character's Wear Total. Any armour in
excess of this value is ignored. A Character may
increase their Wear Total by purchasing the Skill
Wear Extra Armour.
If a Character has more than one type of armour
their maximum Wear Total applies to the combined
value of all armour the character has. The Character
chooses the order in which their armour is damaged
by attacks.
Refitting Armour
A Character may recover all lost Armour Points by
performing a special sixty(60) second Count. The
Role-playing requirements for this Count are
described in the individual Armour Types described
below. A Character can perform the Counts to Refit
multiple Armour Types as part of the same count. The
Character may also perform this Counted Action
while walking and speaking but cannot do so if they
are struck by any effect or use any Skill.
Arcane Armour
This Armour is composed of magical energy which
surrounds and protects the wearer. If the Character is
struck by a Dispel Magic effect, any remaining
Armour Points from Arcane Armour the character has
are lost and the Character is reduced to zero(0)
Arcane Armour points until they Refit. Arcane Armour
can be Refit by the Character spending a sixty(60)-
second count concentrating, by the Refit Arcane
Armour effect, or the Refit All Armour effect.
Avoidance Armour
This Armour is a Character's ability to roll with
attacks and otherwise avoid injury. If the Character is
struck by any effect which immobilises them such as;
Confine, Paralysis, or Web, any remaining Armour
Points from Avoidance Armour the character has are
lost and the Character is reduced to zero(0)
Avoidance Armour points until they Refit. Avoidance
Armour can be Refit by the Character spending a
sixty(60) second count stretching, by the Refit
Avoidance Armour effect, or the Refit All Armour
effect.
Natural Armour
This Armour is a Character's natural protective
armour that is part of their body. Natural Armour can
be Refit by the Character spending a sixty(60)
second count role-playing the repair of the armour in
any way they see fit, for example applying oils and
lotions, moulting off old plates and so on. It can also
be Refit by the Refit Natural Armour effect, or the
Refit All Armour effect. Natural Armour cannot be
Broken or Destroyed.
Physical Armour
This armour is any which is manufactured and
created by the Smithing Skill. It is the most common
form of armour being made of toughened hide or
metal. It comes in a variety of forms and grants
Armour Points based on its material and the parts of
the body it covers. In order for armour to be
considered covering an area it must cover a total of
75% of the total area. The Armour Points granted by
coverage of each area can be found in the table
below. As an item, Physical Armour is a valid target
for the Break and Destroy effects. Both of these
effects instantly reduce the number of Armour Points
to zero(0) Armour Points. Physical Armour which is
Indestructible is not reduced to zero(0) Armour
Points. Physical Armour is Refit by spending a
sixty(60)-second count role-playing adjusting straps,
plates and armour sections in order to ensure proper
coverage. It can also be Refit by the Refit Physical
Armour effect, or the Refit All Armour effect.
Armour Location Leather Mail Plate
Head (Scalp) 2 4 6
Front Torso 3 6 9
Back Torso 3 6 9
Upper Arm (Each) 2 3 4
Forearm (Each) 1 2 4
Upper Leg (Each) 2 3 4
Lower Leg (Each) 1 2 4
Table 6.2 – Armour Locations
Fortification Points
Fortification Points are a form of temporary
protection which extends over a Character's body
protecting both Armour and Hit Points from damage.
Whenever a Character is affected by an effect which
causes damage the damage is first subtracted from
any Fortification Points the Character may have. Any
damage which exceeds any Fortification Points the
Character may have causes damage to Armour
Points, or Hit Points, as appropriate.
Unless explicitly stated otherwise, no abilities
which grant Fortification Points stack, instead any
Fortify effect simply overlaps with any current
Fortification Points the Character possesses. For
example, if a Character has three(3) Fortification
Points and if affected by a "Magic Fortify 5" effect the
Character will have five(5) Fortification Points. If a
Character has ten(10) Fortification Points and if
affected by a "Magic Fortify 5" effect the Character
still has ten(10) Fortification Points.
Hit Points
Hit Points represent how much damage a
Character can endure before falling Unconscious.
Damage is usually done to Armour Points before Hit
Points but some effects do damage to Hit Points
directly. Effects which cause Body damage such as
Inflict Wounds spells and Cause Damage Alchemy
damage a target's Hit Points bypassing any armour.
All Player Characters start with ten(10) Hit Points.
This value can be increased by the Toughness Skill.
Hit Points are restored by Healing effects. Each
point of Healing restored one(1) lost Hit Point. Any
amount of Healing which would restore more Hit
Points than the Character is missing is lost unless the
Character has an ability which states otherwise, such
as the Narahk's Runic Flesh ability.
The States of of Living
Conscious
The normal state of a Character, with one(1) or
more Hit Points is Conscious. The Character is aware
of their surroundings.
Unconscious
A Character is Unconscious if they are at zero(0)
Hit Points. An Unconscious Character is helpless and
unaware of their surroundings. After one(1) minute,
the Character regains one(1) Hit Point regaining
Consciousness. An Unconscious Character cannot
use any Skills, or Defensive Abilities which are
considered Choice Defences such as Ward, Dodge
or Resist.
Dying
Also called Bleeding Out, Character is Dying if they
are reduced to below zero(0) Hit Points. A Dying
Character is at -1 Hit Points. Excess damage is not
tracked. A Dying Character is helpless and unaware
of their surroundings. As soon as a Character begins
Dying they begin a sixty(60) second Bleed Out Count.
At the end of this count the Character becomes
Dead. A Dying Character cannot use any Skills, or
Defensive Abilities which are considered Choice
Defences such as Ward, Dodge or Resist.
A Dying Character can be Stabilised by the Skill
First Aid, or by a Stabilise effect. Once Stabilised the
character is at zero(0) Hit Points and becomes
Unconscious. If a Dying Character receives any
amount of Healing they are no longer Dying and
Conscious.
For example, Ills has 4 Hit Points when they are
struck by a spell causing 10 points of damage. Ills is
now Dying and after one minute will be Dead. But Ills
receives 10 points of Healing after a few seconds. Ills
is now at 9 Hit Points and able to act normally.
Dead
A Character which is Dead is helpless and
unaware of their surroundings. As soon as a
Character becomes Dead they begin a five(5) minute
Death Count. If the Character's Death Count ends the
Character immediately dissipates becoming a Spirit.
Once a Character has become a Spirit they must
leave any Game Items at the location of their body,
put on a red headband and walk to a Circle of
Resurrection. The Player chooses which Circle the
Character travels to. A Dead Character cannot use
any Skills, or Defensive Abilities which are considered
Choice Defences such as Ward, Dodge or Resist.
Dissipated

A Player whose Character has dissipated and
become a spirit must report to Plot so their death may
be recorded. Additionally, if the Character's death
adds a Death to their Character, the Player must
draw from the Bag of Death. This bag contains
ten(10) white stones. For each time the Character
has Resurrected, after the first, one of the white
stones will be replaced with a black stone. If the
Player draws a white stone the Character resurrects
as normal. If however the Player draws a black stone
the Character suffers one of the fates outlined in
Table 6.3 – Fates.
Fate Name Effect
Soulflay The Character returns to life and their number of Character Deaths is
reduced to zero(0). However, the Character is reduced to 70% of their
previous experience point total.
Reaper's Grasp The Character returns to life but a looming fate awaits them. The Character
may be destined to die in an imminent battle, or they may have some
unfinished business. Either way, at the end of the next Event the Character
attends the character is considered to be Permanently Dead. After this time,
the Player may create a new Character with 75% of the Permanently Dead
Character's Experience.
Insurmountable Trauma The Character returns to life but retires from adventuring. At the end of the
event the Character is considered to be Permanently Dead. After this time,
the Player may create a new Character with 80% of the Permanently Dead
Character's Experience.
Permanent Death The Character fails to resurrect. At this time, the Player may create a new
Character with 80% of the Permanently Dead Character's Experience.
Table 6.3 – Fates
Resurrections
A Character who has Dissipated, had their Death
recorded by Plot, and approved for Resurrection may,
as a Spirit, travel to a Circle of Resurrection. Any
Character, who is Invested in the Circle of
Resurrection, may perform a Resurrection.
Resurrections are a special five(5)-minute counted
action during which the Character may perform a
Guided Resurrection where they role-play guiding a
Spirit back to life. Alternatively, a Character may
perform an Unguided Resurrection, relying instead on
the Circle to bear the load of the resurrection. Once
an unguided resurrection has begun, it continues on
its own and the Character who started the
Resurrection may begin another one. This process is
physically, and mentally, draining on the spirit,
bordering on traumatic. Though there are no in-game
effects for either method, the resurrected character is
free to role-play as they deem appropriate.
If the Spirit is Invested in the Circle of Resurrection
they may force the Circle to resurrect them without
another character's assistance. This process is every
bit as draining as an Unguided Resurrection.
Once the five(5) minute Resurrection count has
finished the Character returns to life, their body
reforming over a special three(3)-count. During this
three(3)-count the Character is Invulnerable to all
effects. A newly Resurrected Character regains all
expended uses of all per Reset and per Day Skills. A
Resurrected Character always has their full Hit
Points.
Carrying a Character
Most Characters can be carried by another but
doing so is difficult. In order to role-play this a
Character must have both hands free, and cannot be
holding anything with either arm. They state to their
target, who is usually helpless, "I am carrying you.".
The target must then stand in front of the Character
"Carrying them". The Character doing the carrying
places both hands above the target's shoulders and
both Characters walk together. It is never possible to
run while carrying a Character.
Once one Character is carrying another, should
either Character be struck by any effect delivered by
weapon or packet, even if it is negated, the carried
Character is "dropped" and the carrying Character
must wait ten(10) seconds before attempting to carry
them again. Effects which strike a Character carrying
or being carried only applies to whoever was struck.
It is possible for two Characters to carry the same
target, in this case each Character needs to have
only one free hand each, with each carrying
Character holding one hand above the target.
Some creatures cannot be carried by Player
Characters.
Standard Abilities and Effects
The following is a reference list of all standard
effects in Crucible.
Abolishment – This effects removes all beneficial
effects from the target. This includes all Techniques with
a duration of Concentration, Charged or Sustained. It
also includes all Fortification Points, Shields, Wards,
Absorbs, Imbued Skills, Invocations as well as the
effects; Aegis, Ambush, Mirage, Vorpal, and <Type>
Aura.
Absorb <Effect> – This effect allows the user to Absorb
a single effect negating its normal effects and instead
causing a different one. The specific effects of the
Absorb ability vary by creature or effect which grants this
ability. The call for this ability is "Absorb".
Aegis – This effect, typically, renders the user Immune
to Weapon Damage and effects delivered as <Effect>-
Strikes. An Aegis is represented by the Character
crossing both arms over their upper chest. While using
this ability, unless states otherwise, the Character
cannot move faster than a normal walking pace and
cannot use any weapon or packet delivered attacks
except on Touch Casting on themselves. The call for this
ability is "No Effect".
Alarm – This effect can only be placed on a Room.
Should any creature, not invested in the Alarm effect,
enter the room they, or the accompanying Marshal,
must knock loudly for ten(10) seconds. The purpose
of this effect is to alert the occupants of the room, not
to prevent the intruder from defending themselves in
any way. The intruder may still engage in other
activities while they are knocking. An Alarm must be
included in the Marshal Notes for the affected Room.
The points of entry of the room must be marked with
a Stylized "A" or "W".
Alchemy Conversion – This ability allows a
Character to expend an Alchemy Slot of any level in
order to regain a Alchemy Slot of any level below that
of the expended Alchemy Slot.
Ambush <#> – This ability grants a number of charges
of the Ambush effect to the target equal to <#>. In order
